- Evidence: src/core/apfs_keybag.cpp:79-91. `int len` accumulates long-form DER length via `len = (len << 8) | byte` (line 84) with n up to 127 (line 81). Four+ length bytes with the top bit set overflow signed int and produce a negative len (UB on the sign-bit shift). The only bound, `if (i + len > buf.size())` (line 87), does not catch negative len, so `i += len` (line 91) drives i negative; the loop guard `i + 2 <= buf.size()` (line 77) stays true and `buf.at(i++)` (line 78) then reads at a negative index -> OOB read / assert crash. Reachable from parseKeyBlob (lines 145/150/155) on on-disk keybag blobs, i.e. untrusted/foreign-image data. No upstream validation guards it.

### P06-04 [MEDIUM] src/core/app_scanner.cpp:142
**Registry string can cause out-of-bounds read**

- Trigger: A REG_SZ value under an Uninstall subkey (e.g. DisplayName) written as exactly 1024 wide chars with no terminating NUL (data length == sizeof(buffer), so RegQueryValueExW still returns ERROR_SUCCESS rather than ERROR_MORE_DATA). QString::fromWCharArray then runs wcslen off the end of buffer, reading uninitialized/out-of-bounds stack memory until it happens on a zero word -> OOB read (crash or garbage/leaked bytes appended to the app name). Fix: use RegGetValueW (which guarantees termination) or pass the returned length: QString::fromWCharArray(buffer, bufferSize/sizeof(wchar_t)) after trimming a trailing NUL.
- Evidence: src/core/app_scanner.cpp:142-154. buffer is declared `wchar_t buffer[kRegistryValueBufferChars]` (kRegistryValueBufferChars=1024, line 24) with NO zero-initialization. RegQueryValueExW (line 146-151) fills it and updates bufferSize to the actual byte length, but on ERROR_SUCCESS the code calls the single-argument `QString::fromWCharArray(buffer)` (line 154), which uses wcslen to find the length and completely ignores the returned bufferSize. Per Microsoft's RegQueryValueExW docs, a REG_SZ/REG_EXPAND_SZ value stored without a trailing NUL is returned verbatim without one appended. In that case fromWCharArray scans past the written data into uninitialized stack, and if the value occupies all 1024 wchars with no NUL, it reads out of bounds past buffer[1023]. Nothing in the current tree bounds the conversion or NUL-terminates the buffer, so the report accurately describes current behavior.
- Fix: Replace single-arg fromWCharArray with a length-bounded conversion using bufferSize, or switch to RegGetValueW which guarantees NUL termination.

### P07-06 [MEDIUM] src/core/bandwidth_tester.cpp:243
**Failed iPerf startup can leave an inbound firewall rule**

- Trigger: User starts the iPerf server; isIperf3Available() passes (iperf3.exe exists at check time, line 233/208), so createFirewallRule adds the SAK_Utility_iPerf3 inbound TCP allow rule for the chosen port. QProcess::start() then fails (e.g., the executable is blocked by antivirus, lacks execute permission, or is corrupted), raising QProcess::FailedToStart. The handler at lines 279-287 tears down m_serverProcess without calling removeFirewallRule(), leaving the inbound TCP port permanently allowed through the Windows firewall.
- Evidence: src/core/bandwidth_tester.cpp:243 calls createFirewallRule(port) (adds inbound TCP allow rule 'SAK_Utility_iPerf3' via netsh) BEFORE m_serverProcess->start() at line 289. The FailedToStart handler at lines 279-287 emits the error and does m_serverProcess->deleteLater()/m_serverProcess=nullptr but never calls removeFirewallRule(). removeFirewallRule() is only invoked in the QProcess::finished handler (line 271), and Qt does not emit finished() when a process fails to start. So on FailedToStart the inbound allow rule is never removed. Destructor cleanup does not help either: ~BandwidthTester (line 179) -> stopIperfServer() returns early at line 293-295 because m_serverProcess was already set to nullptr by the FailedToStart handler. The rule leaks until a later successful server run happens to call removeFirewallRule().
- Fix: Call createFirewallRule only after QProcess::started fires, and call removeFirewallRule() in the FailedToStart handler (and unconditionally in stopIperfServer before the null-guard early return).

### P05-01 [MEDIUM] src/core/dbx_writer.cpp:156
**Generated DBX files are structurally invalid**

- Trigger: Export any single email via DbxWriter::writeMessage, then finalize(). The resulting .dbx contains a header + length-prefixed RFC5322 blobs but no message-index B-tree, so opening it in Outlook Express (or any DBX-conformant reader) yields an empty/unreadable folder - the stored messages cannot be enumerated or imported.
- Evidence: src/core/dbx_writer.cpp:155-183 writeDbxHeader emits only a fixed-size header (magic kDbxFolderMagic, version marker, 6 reserved u32, folder-name offset/len, message-count placeholder @0x24, folder name @0x68). buildDbxMessageEntry (185-209) then appends each message as a bare 4-byte LE length prefix + RFC5322 bytes concatenated directly after the header. There is NO OE5/6 message-index tree: no tree-root pointer, no node segments, no per-message offset table. A conforming Outlook Express DBX reader locates messages via that index, so it cannot enumerate/import this output. The code comment itself concedes: "Real OE5/6 DBX has a complex B-tree structure; we write a minimal header". Nothing in finalize() (132-149) builds an index either; finalize only patches the message count at 0x24. Report's structural claim is accurate and unguarded.
- Fix: Either implement the full OE5/6 DBX index tree (message info records + node segments + tree-root pointers) so readers can locate messages, or remove/relabel the DBX export until the conforming format is written. No data is lost today (RFC5322 content is raw-recoverable), so severity is MEDIUM not HIGH.

### P07-12 [MEDIUM] src/core/diagnostic_report_generator.cpp:124
**Truncated HTML and CSV reports are reported successful**

- Trigger: Call generateHtml() (or generateCsv()) with output_path on a nearly-full volume or a removable/network drive that becomes unavailable during the write. The QTextStream write fails or flushes only partial content on file.close(), yet the method emits reportGenerated("HTML"/"CSV", output_path) and returns true, so the caller treats a truncated/empty report file as a successful, complete report.
- Evidence: generateHtml (src/core/diagnostic_report_generator.cpp:113-130) and generateCsv (324-348) both write through QTextStream then call file.close() and immediately Q_EMIT reportGenerated(...) and return true. Neither checks QTextStream::status() nor QFile::error()/QFileDevice status after writing. QTextStream buffers internally; a mid-write failure (disk full, removable device unplugged) is not propagated. The JSON path (writeJsonDocument, 76-82) proves the codebase knows to check writes (file.write()!=data.size()), but HTML/CSV do not. No guard, validation, or QSaveFile is present on these two paths.
- Fix: After writing, call stream.flush() then require stream.status()==QTextStream::Ok and file.error()==QFileDevice::NoError (or use QSaveFile with commit()) before emitting reportGenerated/returning true; on failure emit errorOccurred and return false, matching writeJsonDocument's pattern.

### P03-14 [MEDIUM] src/core/drive_unmounter.cpp:290
**Disk is left persistently offline**

- Trigger: Flash any target that enumerates as a fixed disk (e.g. a USB SSD, or any disk whose persisted offline attribute sticks). unmountVolumes -> unmountDrive -> preventAutoMount sets DISK_ATTRIBUTE_OFFLINE with Persist=TRUE. After the write finishes (or fails), nothing re-onlines the disk, so the freshly-flashed drive does not appear in File Explorer / normal volume access. Because Persist=TRUE writes the offline state to the SAN-policy persistent store keyed by the disk identifier, the drive stays offline across unplug/replug and reboot; the user must manually run diskpart 'select disk N' -> 'online disk' to recover it. Data on the disk is intact but the volume is inaccessible until manual intervention.
- Evidence: drive_unmounter.cpp:288-292 sets SET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES with Attributes=DISK_ATTRIBUTE_OFFLINE and Persist=TRUE, applied via IOCTL_DISK_SET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES (lines 295-302). The disk is taken offline and the state is persisted across reboots. Grep of the whole tree shows this is the ONLY IOCTL_DISK_SET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES call site; there is no counterpart that clears the offline attribute or brings the disk online, and no saved-original-attribute restore. The flash completion/cleanup paths (flash_coordinator.cpp onWorkerCompleted line 251, cleanupWorkers line 438-461) only stop workers and close the image source. DriveUnmounter's destructor (drive_unmounter.cpp:62-70) only closes locked-volume handles. unmountDrive (line 72) calls preventAutoMount at line 85 as a fire-and-forget step whose only failure handling is a warning. So on both success and failure paths the target disk is left persistently offline.
- Fix: Read the disk's current attributes (or at minimum track that this code set it offline) and clear DISK_ATTRIBUTE_OFFLINE via IOCTL_DISK_SET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES after the flash completes on every completion and failure path; alternatively set Persist=FALSE so the offline state does not survive reboots and re-online the disk in a finalize step.

### P06-05 [MEDIUM] src/core/elevated_pipe_server.cpp:42
**Named-pipe connection timeout cannot fire**

- Trigger: Parent process launches the UAC-elevated helper (passing its PID as m_parent_pid), then the parent is killed or crashes before it opens the pipe via CreateFile. In the helper, start() -> waitForPipeClient() -> ConnectNamedPipe blocks synchronously forever, ignoring the OVERLAPPED and kPipeConnectTimeoutMs, leaving an orphaned elevated helper process hung indefinitely instead of timing out and exiting.
- Evidence: src/core/elevated_pipe_server.cpp:42-49 createServerPipe() calls CreateNamedPipeW with dwOpenMode = PIPE_ACCESS_DUPLEX only, i.e. NO FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED, so the handle is synchronous. src/core/elevated_pipe_server.cpp:52-75 waitForPipeClient() passes an OVERLAPPED (with event) to ConnectNamedPipe (line 60) and only applies the kPipeConnectTimeoutMs timeout in the ERROR_IO_PENDING branch (lines 64-68). Per Microsoft docs, on a synchronous (non-OVERLAPPED) pipe handle ConnectNamedPipe blocks until a client connects or an error occurs and never returns ERROR_IO_PENDING; the lpOverlapped is ignored. Thus the timeout branch is unreachable dead code and the intended connect-timeout can never fire. No watchdog, alertable wait, or parent-liveness check guards this.
- Fix: Create the pipe with FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED in createServerPipe (dwOpenMode |= FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED), and complete the ConnectNamedPipe operation properly (wait on the event with kPipeConnectTimeoutMs, then CancelIo on timeout). All subsequent ReadFile/WriteFile must then also use OVERLAPPED, or a separate synchronous handle path must be maintained.

### P06-06 [MEDIUM] src/core/elevation_manager.cpp:102
**Elevated relaunch corrupts command-line arguments**

- Trigger: Run the app unelevated with argument --accessibility-audit-output=C:\My Reports\ (a path value containing a space and a trailing backslash), then hit the elevation path (restartElevated). arg contains a space so it is serialized as "--accessibility-audit-output=C:\My Reports\". On re-parse the trailing \" is treated as an escaped literal quote, so the closing quote is consumed as data: the elevated process receives the corrupted single argument --accessibility-audit-output=C:\My Reports" (spurious quote appended), and any following argument would be absorbed into it. Empty arguments likewise vanish and tab-bearing arguments split.
- Evidence: src/core/elevation_manager.cpp:94-108 (get_command_line_args): every argv token is re-serialized with the only rule being `if (arg.find(L' ') != npos) args += "\"" + arg + "\"";`. There is no Windows argv quoting: backslashes preceding the closing quote are not doubled, embedded quotes are not escaped, empty arguments produce nothing, and tab-containing (originally quoted) arguments are emitted unquoted. The serialized string is then passed to executeElevated/ShellExecuteExW (line 143/146). The main app accepts value-bearing options whose value can contain spaces and path separators, e.g. --accessibility-audit-output=<path> (main.cpp:399, via rawArgumentValue at main.cpp:100-103), so such tokens genuinely flow through this routine on elevated relaunch. Not guarded anywhere; this is the naive implementation.
- Fix: Replace the space-only quoting with a complete Windows argv serializer per the CommandLineToArgvW rules: always wrap each argument in quotes (or when it contains space/tab/quote/is empty), double any run of backslashes that immediately precedes a quote or the closing quote, and escape embedded quotes as \".

### P05-02 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_attachment_saver.cpp:49
**Short attachment writes report success**

- Trigger: On ENOSPC/disk-full or an I/O fault mid-write, file.write() writes fewer bytes than data.size() (or close() fails to flush), leaving a truncated or empty attachment on disk while saveAttachmentToPath returns {true, path, {}}; the batch reports "Saved N attachment(s)" and the user believes the corrupt file was saved intact.
- Evidence: src/core/email_attachment_saver.cpp:49-51 in saveAttachmentToPath: `file.write(data); file.close(); return {true, path, {}};`. The write() return value is discarded, close() (which flushes and can report write failures) is unchecked, and the function unconditionally returns success once open() succeeds. No QSaveFile, no size comparison, no flush/close error propagation. This is the only write path; both saveAttachmentToDirectory (line 76) and AttachmentBatchSave::recordOne (line 95) route through it, and recordOne increments m_succeeded on result.success, so a truncated file is counted as saved and summaryText() reports full success.
- Fix: Check file.write(data) == data.size() and file.close() success (or use QSaveFile with commit()); on mismatch remove()/rollback the partial file and return {false, path, error}.

### P05-04 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_export_worker.cpp:167
**Whole-folder exports stop after 500 items**

- Trigger: Export an entire MBOX or PST folder that contains 501+ messages without supplying explicit item_ids (config.item_ids empty, config.folder_id set). Only the first 500 messages are written; items 501..N are silently omitted with no error or warning.

The folder's real item count is read separately from content_count (pst_parser.cpp:953), confirming folders can exceed 500. Neither enumerator pages by advancing offset, so whole-folder exports (empty item_ids + nonzero folder_id) omit every item after the 500th. No guard exists.
- Fix: Page the enumeration: loop readMessages/readFolderItems advancing offset by kMaxItemsPerLoad until the parser returns fewer than kMaxItemsPerLoad (or empty) items, accumulating all indices/ids.

### P05-07 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_export_worker.cpp:1180
**Partial attachment extraction is counted as complete**

- Trigger: Attachments-format export of a PST message with 3 attachments where attachment[0] extracts fine but attachment[1] and [2] fail (e.g. readAttachmentData returns nullopt on a corrupt/unreadable block, or QFile::open fails). exportAttachments returns true, the item increments items_exported with items_failed=0 and no error string, so the user is told the export fully succeeded while 2 of 3 attachments are silently missing on disk.
- Evidence: src/core/email_export_worker.cpp:1180-1204 exportAttachments() uses `bool any_success = false;` and sets it true if ANY extractAttachment() call succeeds, then `return any_success;`. The caller exportPerItemFormats() at lines 427-431 does `if (exportOnePstItem(...)) { result.items_exported++; } else { result.items_failed++; }`, and exportOnePstItem dispatches to exportAttachments for the Attachments format (lines 478-480). extractAttachment (965-998) silently returns false on readAttachmentData nullopt (971) or file.open failure (993) and never appends to result.errors, so a per-attachment failure is invisible. Net: a message with several attachments where one succeeds and others fail is counted as fully exported (items_exported++), no items_failed, no error logged.
- Fix: Track eligible (non-skipped/filtered) attachment count and successful count; return true only when every eligible attachment succeeded, and append an error listing failed attachment names so the item is recorded as failed/partial rather than complete.

### P05-08 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_export_worker.cpp:952
**Exported CSV permits spreadsheet formula injection**

It does not neutralize spreadsheet formula-leading characters (= + - @ tab CR). writeCsv (952) and csvFieldValue (1211) pass raw untrusted PST field content (subject, sender, body preview) straight through. A value like =HYPERLINK("http://evil","x") contains no quote-triggering char, so it is emitted verbatim and unquoted.

### P05-09 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_export_worker.cpp:324
**vCard and iCalendar fields permit record injection**

Only DESCRIPTION (l.899-906) escapes \\ \n , ; -- proving inconsistent omission. Field values come from parsed PST item detail, no upstream CRLF/delimiter sanitization.
- Fix: Apply RFC 6350 (vCard) / RFC 5545 (iCalendar) TEXT escaping to every emitted value: escape \\ , ; and newline, strip/encode bare CR and LF, and apply 75-octet line folding. Route N:, FN, SUMMARY, LOCATION, ATTENDEE CN through the same escaper already used for DESCRIPTION.

### P05-14 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_profile_manager.cpp:259

### P05-15 [MEDIUM] src/core/email_search_worker.cpp:93

### P05-17 [MEDIUM] src/core/eml_writer.cpp:25

### P06-07 [MEDIUM] src/core/encryption.cpp:284
**Encrypted backups have no authentication**

- Trigger: Attacker with write access to an encrypted backup file flips bits in the stored IV (offset salt_size..salt_size+iv_size) or ciphertext. decryptData at line 326-334 runs AES-CBC decrypt; a modified IV deterministically XOR-alters the first recovered plaintext block while final-block PKCS#7 padding stays valid, so BCryptDecrypt succeeds, plaintext is non-empty, and the tampered content is returned as authentic with no error detected.
- Evidence: src/core/encryption.cpp:284 encryptData emits exactly [salt][IV][ciphertext] with no MAC/tag; aes_encrypt (line 140) uses BCRYPT_CHAIN_MODE_CBC (line 105) + BCRYPT_BLOCK_PADDING, i.e. unauthenticated AES-256-CBC. decryptData (line 299) / aes_decrypt (line 194) accept any successfully-padded output as valid (line 331) with no keyed integrity check. No AES-GCM, HMAC, or encrypt-then-MAC exists anywhere in the path. The claim of no authentication is accurate and unguarded.
- Fix: Adopt a versioned AEAD format (AES-256-GCM via BCRYPT_CHAIN_MODE_GCM storing nonce+tag) or encrypt-then-HMAC-SHA256 over salt||IV||ciphertext with a separate PBKDF2-derived MAC key, and verify the tag before decrypting.

Every other route in the same file preserves warnings (compress at line 54, deliverTree bridge import at 161, transfer engine at 184/200), proving warning propagation is the intended contract. extractZip DOES emit warnings: extractZipEntry (file_explorer_archive_service.cpp:83-86) appends "Skipped symlink %1 (links are not extracted)." and returns true, so the symlink entry is silently skipped while extractZip returns result.ok=true (line 271). Result: those skip warnings are dropped only on the extract paths.
- Fix: On each extractZip consumption at worker lines ~122/135/151 add m_warnings.append(result.warnings) alongside the existing m_blockers.append(result.blockers), matching the compress path at line 54.

### P04-22 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_explorer_group_proxy_model.cpp:82
**Persistent indexes are captured after source reordering**

- Trigger: Grouped view (m_option != None) with file X selected at proxy row R (source row S). User clicks a column header (or otherwise triggers the underlying sort proxy to re-sort) such that after the sort, source row S now holds a different file Y. On layoutChanged, mapToSource(R) reads source row S (=Y) and the selection/current index is remapped from X onto Y.
- Evidence: file_explorer_group_proxy_model.cpp:79-93. The source persistent-index snapshot (mapToSource at lines 85-87) is built inside the layoutChanged slot, i.e. AFTER the underlying sort proxy has already reordered its rows. mapToSource (216-227) resolves the OLD m_rows source_row (rebuildGroups runs later at line 88) into sourceModel()->index(old_source_row, col); since the source already re-sorted, that row number now holds a different item, so the QPersistentModelIndex binds to the wrong item. changePersistentIndex (line 90) then moves the view's selection/current index onto that different file. The Qt-canonical fix (as QSortFilterProxyModel does) is to snapshot source QPersistentModelIndexes in the layoutAboutToBeChanged slot (line 76-78), where OLD m_rows is still valid AND the source row still maps to the original item, then remap in layoutChanged. The path is live: a plain re-sort (details-view header click -> sort() line 318 -> sourceModel()->sort()) emits layoutAboutToBeChanged/layoutChanged rather than a reset.
- Fix: Move the source-index capture (persistentIndexList + mapToSource -> QPersistentModelIndex) into the layoutAboutToBeChanged slot, store it as a member, and in layoutChanged call rebuildGroups() then changePersistentIndex using the stored snapshot.

### P04-23 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_explorer_group_proxy_model.cpp:100
**Data changes leave items under stale group headers**

- Trigger: Set grouping to Tag and keep the default Name sort. Right-click a file whose tags are empty and add a tag via the tag context menu. refreshTags() emits a plain dataChanged(EntryTagsRole); the Name-sorted sort-filter proxy forwards it without layoutChanged; the group proxy forwards the repaint but does not rebuildGroups(), so the file remains under the old "No tags" group header instead of moving under the new tag's header until the folder is reloaded or re-sorted.
- Evidence: Model chain confirmed in file_explorer_pane.cpp:45-53 - FileExplorerItemModel -> FileExplorerSortFilterModel (sorted by NameColumn at line 49) -> FileExplorerGroupProxyModel. The group proxy's dataChanged handler (file_explorer_group_proxy_model.cpp:94-114) only remaps and re-emits the repaint; it never calls rebuildGroups(), and its comment (lines 100-101) asserts group membership only changes on re-sort/reset. That assumption is false for FileTag grouping: toggling a tag (file_management_explorer_panel.cpp:7150-7155) calls FileExplorerItemModel::refreshTags(), which emits a plain dataChanged for {Qt::DisplayRole, EntryTagsRole} (file_explorer_item_model.cpp:432-434). rebuildGroups() keys each row via EntryTagsRole (line 189/uses group_source.tags from EntryTagsRole at line 189 of grouping path -> proxy line 189). Because the sort key is Name, the sort-filter proxy does not re-sort and forwards a plain dataChanged (no layoutChanged), so the group proxy never regroups. FileTag grouping is a real option (grouping.h FileExplorerGroupOption::FileTag; menu entry at panel line 718). Result: the item stays under its stale tag-group header until the next directory reload/reset/re-sort. Not a data-safety issue; self-heals on reload, but visibly defeats the group-by-tag feature while active.

### P04-24 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_hash.cpp:109

ASCII paths coincide so the common case works, but any non-ASCII path is mangled (or path::string() throws std::system_error, caught at line 130). The mangled QString does not name the real file, so QFile::open at line 110 fails and the call returns error_code::read_error. The same defect appears at line 111 logError(... file_path.string()). No wide-path handling exists in the tree; fix is QString::fromStdWString(file_path.native()).
- Fix: Replace QString::fromStdString(file_path.string()) with QString::fromStdWString(file_path.native()) at line 109 (and use path native/QString for the log at 111) so the Windows wide path is passed to QFile without a lossy narrow round-trip.

### P04-30 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:1467

### P03-13 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_recovery_engine.cpp:350

Fix: require write() to equal bytes.size() and check close() success before recording the path (ideally via QSaveFile so a failed write leaves no partial file).
- Evidence: src/core/file_recovery_engine.cpp:344-353 writeRecoveredFile discards the return value of output.write(bytes) (line 350) and output.close() (line 351), then unconditionally returns true (line 352). restoreCandidate (lines 366-369) appends outputPath to result->restored_paths on that true. candidateBytesMatch validates only the in-memory buffer before the write, not the write itself, so nothing checks that all bytes reached disk.
- Fix: In writeRecoveredFile, compare output.write(bytes) to bytes.size() and check output.close() (or use QSaveFile::commit()); return false and emit a warning on mismatch so the path is not recorded as restored.

### P04-14 [MEDIUM] src/core/file_scanner.cpp:322
**Symlink directories are followed when disabled**

- Trigger: Scan a root containing a directory junction/symlink with default scan_options (follow_symlinks=false, recursive=true, max_depth=0): e.g. dir C:\scanroot\link -> C:\Windows. is_directory() returns true, line 322 recurses, and scanDirectoryRecursive opens directory_iterator on C:\scanroot\link which follows into C:\Windows, escaping the intended root. If the junction targets an ancestor (link -> C:\scanroot), the scan recurses without bound until MAX_PATH errors terminate it, effectively hanging the scan.

Thus symlinked directories are traversed even when disabled, and an ancestor-pointing link recurses without a visited-set bound.

### P07-17 [MEDIUM] src/core/firewall_rule_auditor.cpp:120
**Non-overlapping firewall rules are reported as conflicts**

- Trigger: Two enabled inbound rules with opposite actions produce a false conflict: (a) a GRE rule (protocol 47, empty ports) mapped to Any overlapping a TCP Block rule; and (b) a Domain-only Allow rule (profiles=1) vs a Public-only Block rule (profiles=4) on the same port, which never apply simultaneously yet are reported as conflicting because rulesOverlap ignores the profile bitmask and address scopes.
- Evidence: protocolFromNumber (src/core/firewall_rule_auditor.cpp:120-133) maps any non-{TCP,UDP,ICMPv4,ICMPv6} protocol (e.g. GRE=47) to Protocol::Any via the default case. protocolsOverlap (431-436) returns true whenever either side is Any, so a GRE-derived Any rule overlaps a TCP rule. rulesOverlap (445-459) is the only conflict gate and checks enabled/direction/action/protocol/ports/appPath but NEVER inspects rule.profiles (bitmask) or localAddresses/remoteAddresses. Because empty ports make portsOverlap return true (line 665) and empty app paths make applicationPathsMatch true (438-443), disjoint rules are flagged. findConflicts (487-503) feeds every rulesOverlap hit into buildConflict.
- Fix: Preserve the numeric protocol (or add an explicit Other/GRE value) so unknown protocols do not collapse to Any, and extend rulesOverlap to require profile-mask intersection ((a.profiles & b.profiles) != 0) and local/remote address overlap in addition to the existing port/protocol/app checks.

### P05-21 [MEDIUM] src/core/imap_uploader.cpp:331
**Immediate APPEND rejection is ignored**

- Trigger: Server rejects an APPEND immediately per RFC 3501/4469 with a tagged response and no continuation, e.g. `A0001 NO [TOOBIG] message too large\r\n` or `A0001 NO [TRYCREATE]\r\n` or an over-quota `A0001 NO ...`. The buffer contains no '+', handleContinuation short-circuits, handleAppendResponse is never invoked, and instead of marking that one message failed and continuing (++failed, ++m_currentIndex, appendNext), the entire session times out and fails.
- Evidence: imap_uploader.cpp handleContinuation (lines 331-343): `if (!m_waitingContinuation || !m_buffer.contains("+")) return m_waitingContinuation;`. When m_waitingContinuation is true (set in sendAppendCommand line 178) and the buffer lacks a '+', it returns true, so handleReadable (lines 310-312) returns before handleTaggedResponse() (line 313/345). The tagged callback handleAppendResponse (line 205) never runs, m_waitingContinuation stays true, and the session only exits via the timeout timer -> failConnection("IMAP operation timed out") -> finish(false), aborting the whole upload. No alternate path parses the tagged response while awaiting continuation.
- Fix: While m_waitingContinuation is true, before returning, check whether m_buffer contains m_currentTag (a tagged NO/BAD/OK) and if so route to handleTaggedResponse instead of waiting for a '+' continuation.

### P05-22 [MEDIUM] src/core/imap_uploader.cpp:266
**Untagged OK can mask failed authentication**

- Trigger: IMAP server (or MITM) replies to AUTHENTICATE with "* OK [ALERT] password expired\r\nA0001 NO [AUTHENTICATIONFAILED] Authentication failed\r\n". handleAuthResponse sees "OK" from the untagged line, skips the failure path, logs "authenticated", and proceeds to continueAfterAuth/CREATE/APPEND despite the tagged NO result - masking the real auth failure.

An untagged "* OK" line preceding a tagged "A000N NO ..." result satisfies contains("OK") and is treated as authenticated. The correct tagged check already exists at line 206 in handleAppendResponse: response.contains(m_currentTag + " OK"). Same loose-check defect also affects handleNoopResponse (291) and handleCreateResponse (299).
- Fix: Require the tagged result: change line 267 to check response.contains(m_currentTag + " OK") (mirroring line 206); apply the same tagged-match fix to handleNoopResponse (291) and handleCreateResponse (299).

### P05-23 [MEDIUM] src/core/imap_uploader.cpp:235
**IMAP quoted strings are not escaped**

- Trigger: A password containing a double quote, e.g. pa"ss, yields `LOGIN "user" "pa"ss"` which is malformed IMAP and authentication fails. A PST folder name containing CR/LF (e.g. "Inbox\r\nA001 DELETE INBOX") produces an APPEND/CREATE line that terminates early and injects a following IMAP command; a folder name with a bare `"` similarly breaks the command.
- Evidence: In the live ImapSessionWorker path (used by runImapSession), user/config-supplied values are placed directly inside IMAP quoted strings with no escaping and no validation: line 236 `LOGIN "%1" "%2"` (username/password), line 171 `APPEND "%1"` (target_folder), line 286 `CREATE "%1"` (target_folder). RFC 3501 requires quoted strings to backslash-escape `"` and `\`, and forbids CR/LF (which requires a literal). A grep for any escape/replace/literal handling across the file returns nothing. No guard exists. target_folder derives from PST folder names (untrusted source data in a migration tool). The legacy ImapUploader::authenticate/createFolder (lines 554/597) share the defect but are dead code since sendCommand (line 589) always returns unexpected.
- Fix: Add an IMAP quoted-string escaper (backslash-escape `"` and `\`) and reject/encode CR/LF, or use IMAP literals ({n}\r\n form) for username, password, and mailbox names at lines 236, 171, and 286 (and the dead 554/597 if retained).

### P05-24 [MEDIUM] src/core/imap_uploader.cpp:487

- Trigger: Reuse one ImapUploader instance across folders: user cancels during folder A (m_cancelled latches true); the subsequent uploadFolder(config, folderB, ...) call immediately trips appendNext's cancelled->load() check, finishes with operation_cancelled, and returns result.uploaded==0 as a SUCCESS std::expected with no errorOccurred emitted. Caller treats folder B (and every later folder) as successfully uploaded with 0 messages, silently skipping all remaining data.

Entry points testConnection(:446), uploadFolder(:463), connectAndAuth(:504) all pass &m_cancelled into the session without resetting it, so the cancel flag latches permanently on the instance. In uploadFolder the guard at :488 is `if (!result.success && result.error != error_code::operation_cancelled)`; on a cancelled session (success=false, error=operation_cancelled set by worker appendNext :141-144 / cancel timer :361-365) this is false, so control reaches `return result.uploaded;` (:493), returning a valued std::expected<int> (success), not std::unexpected(operation_cancelled). ### P04-32 [MEDIUM] src/core/input_validator.cpp:85
**UTF-8 validation accepts invalid code points**

- Trigger: With require_utf8=true: (1) surrogate ED A0 80 (U+D800) validates as valid; (2) overlong three-byte E0 80 AF encodes '/' (U+002F) and passes, enabling overlong-encoding bypass of downstream character/path checks; (3) out-of-range four-byte F5 80 80 80 (U+140000 > U+10FFFF) and overlong F0 80 80 80 both pass. All are rejected by RFC 3629 but accepted by isValidUtf8.
- Evidence: src/core/input_validator.cpp:85-92 (checkMultiByteUtf8) validates 3- and 4-byte sequences only by continuation-byte shape (hasContinuationBytes), with no lead/second-byte range enforcement. Only the 2-byte branch (line 81) guards overlong via lead>=0xC2. Three-byte branch: any lead 0xE0-0xEF + two 0x80-0xBF passes, so surrogates and overlongs pass. Four-byte branch: mask 0xF8/tag 0xF0 admits leads 0xF0-0xF7, and any three continuations pass, so overlong and >U+10FFFF pass. isValidUtf8 (line 397) calls this, so require_utf8 (line 138) accepts them.
- Fix: In checkMultiByteUtf8, enforce second-byte ranges: 3-byte E0->second 0xA0-0xBF, ED->second 0x80-0x9F (reject surrogates), else 0x80-0xBF; 4-byte restrict lead to 0xF0-0xF4 with F0->second 0x90-0xBF, F4->second 0x80-0x8F, else 0x80-0xBF.

### P07-25 [MEDIUM] src/core/iso_analyzer.cpp:188
**EFI default boot entries are classified as legacy**

- Trigger: An EFI-only bootable ISO whose El Torito boot catalog contains a Validation Entry with Platform ID 0xEF followed by a single Initial/Default Entry (boot indicator 0x88) and no Section Header (0x90/0x91) entries. classifyBootCatalog reads catalog[32]=0x88 != 0xEF so applyDefaultBootEntry sets has_legacy; scanBootCatalogSections finds no section headers so has_efi stays false; result is "Legacy BIOS" for what is actually a UEFI-only image.
- Evidence: src/core/iso_analyzer.cpp:189 applyDefaultBootEntry reads catalog[kDefaultBootCatalogEntryOffset=32] and compares it to kBootMediaEfi=0xEF. Per El Torito, offset 32 is the Initial/Default Entry's byte 0 = Boot Indicator (normally 0x88), NOT a platform/media ID. The EFI platform ID lives in the Validation Entry byte 1 (offset 1), which is never read: scanBootCatalogSections (line 205-219) begins at kFirstCatalogSectionEntry=2 (offset 64), skipping the validation entry, and only inspects Section Header entries (0x90/0x91). classifyBootCatalog (557) combines these two, so a catalog whose EFI is declared in the validation entry with no section header never sets has_efi. bootTypeFromFlags then returns Legacy BIOS. Confirmed no other code path consults the validation-entry platform ID.
- Fix: Read platform ID from Validation Entry byte 1 (catalog[1]) to seed EFI/legacy; treat Initial/Default Entry byte 0 as boot indicator and byte 1 as media type only. Section-header scanning already handles hybrid catalogs correctly.

### P06-08 [MEDIUM] src/core/keep_awake.cpp:20
**Global flag breaks overlapping keep-awake guards**

- Trigger: Start a disk flash (flash_worker, thread A) then start a stress test (stress_test_worker, thread B) so they overlap. Thread A installs the real SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS) and sets s_is_active=true. Thread B's start() early-returns and never installs any request. When the flash finishes first, stop() sets s_is_active=false and clears thread A's request; thread B (still running the stress test) has no keep-awake anywhere, so the system can enter sleep mid-operation. Additionally both threads read/write the plain bool s_is_active concurrently with no synchronization (data race / UB).
- Evidence: keep_awake.h:56 declares `static inline bool s_is_active = false;` - a single non-atomic global with no reference count, despite the header claiming "Thread-Safety: Can be used from any thread". keep_awake.cpp:20 start() early-returns success when s_is_active is already true without calling SetThreadExecutionState for the second caller; the KeepAwakeGuard ctor (cpp:78) still sets m_is_active=true for it. cpp:54 stop() clears the single global and calls SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS) on only the calling thread (a per-thread API). Real overlapping usage exists across worker threads: flash_worker.cpp:80, disk_benchmark_worker.cpp:112, stress_test_worker.cpp:118. Two failure modes: (1) the second concurrent guard installs NO real OS request, so when the first operation finishes and clears the flag, no keep-awake remains while the second long operation continues; (2) concurrent non-atomic read/write of s_is_active from multiple worker threads is a data race (UB). No atomic/mutex/refcount guards this.
- Fix: Replace the single bool with a synchronized reference count (e.g. std::atomic<int> or a mutex-guarded counter): increment/install on first acquire, decrement on release, only call SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS)-to-clear when the count reaches zero; combine requested flags across active guards. ### P07-29 [MEDIUM] src/core/linux_iso_downloader.cpp:591
**Checksum read failure becomes verified download success**

- Trigger: Download finishes and m_savePath exists but cannot be opened ReadOnly at line 554 (e.g. the file is exclusively locked by AV/indexer, was moved, or lost read permission between download and verification). The QtConcurrent lambda returns an empty QString, actual.isEmpty() is true, and the downloader emits downloadComplete instead of downloadError -- an inaccessible/unverifiable artifact is reported as a verified-complete download.
- Evidence: src/core/linux_iso_downloader.cpp:591-597. The hashing lambda at lines 552-564 returns QString() when the just-downloaded file cannot be reopened for reading (QFile::open fails at 554). onChecksumVerified then sees actual.isEmpty() at 591 and, instead of failing, logs a warning, emits statusMessage "Checksum verification skipped (file read error)", sets Phase::Completed, and emits downloadComplete(m_savePath, fileInfo.size()) at 594-595. So an integrity check the user explicitly requested is reported as a successful/complete download when it could not run. This is fail-OPEN, not a known fail-closed blocker. Note the narrow scope: byte-corrupted-but-readable ISOs are still caught by the match==false branch (599-618, which removes the file and emits downloadError); the empty-hash path is only reachable when the file itself is inaccessible (locked/removed/permission), so it does not mask ordinary bit-rot corruption, which is why MEDIUM rather than HIGH.
- Fix: In the actual.isEmpty() branch, set Phase::Failed and emit downloadError (integrity check could not be performed) rather than Phase::Completed + downloadComplete.

### P05-29 [MEDIUM] src/core/mbox_writer.cpp:105

On a case-sensitive APFS volume (standard Apple 'APFS Case-sensitive' format) two sibling dirs 'Foo' and 'foo' can coexist; both fold to the same lowercased visitKey, so the second is treated as already-visited, its subtree is silently skipped, and run() still sets result_.ok = blockers.isEmpty() == true (2237). With the correct extent max(count, newEntries.size()) both maps are <=101 entries and the clear stays <= 4080 < 4096; the overrun exists only because count+mainFqNodes.size() can exceed 101. checkpointMap is exactly blockSize (line 5908 QByteArray(geometry.blockSize,'\0')), and the clear loop (5473-5475) writes via QByteArray::operator[] past size() = out-of-bounds heap write. ### P01-06 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_apfs_writer.cpp:6070
**Cyclic object maps recurse without a guard**

- Trigger: Run an in-place APFS COW commit on a foreign/corrupt container whose physical volume object-map root is a level>0 index node containing a child-paddr value equal to its own (in-range) paddr. readApfsRepairBlock accepts the in-range block, le16(node,level)!=0, so collectLiveOmapTree re-reads the same node and recurses indefinitely until the stack is exhausted and the process crashes.

This is provably an unguarded gap: every sibling B-tree walk in the SAME file carries the guard this one lacks -- collectFsTreeSubtree (7027) has depthBudget{16} (7017) and fails closed at 7032-7036 ("corrupt tree with lying level fields must fail closed, not recurse unboundedly"), the extent-ref walk fails closed at 6503-6519, and the snap-meta walk at 11991-12004. The walk is reached on the in-place COW commit path (resolveLiveVolOmapTree at 6187, commit at 6977), i.e. mutating real/foreign volumes that may be corrupt.
- Fix: Add an int depthBudget (mirroring ApfsFsTreeCollect{depthBudget=16}) to ApfsLiveOmapWalk / the recursion, fail closed with a blocker when it hits 0, and/or track visited paddrs and reject repeats -- matching collectFsTreeSubtree (7032-7036).

### P01-08 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_apfs_writer.cpp:11782

The only guard in the path is a file-SIZE cap (kApfsInPlaceCommitMaxBytes); the offset itself is never bounds-checked. ### P01-10 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_apfs_writer.cpp:17044

A file larger than available RAM fails unconditionally.
- Evidence: partition_apfs_writer.cpp:17044 (collectDirectorySubtree) appends each preserved regular file with .data = QByteArray(static_cast<qsizetype>(entry.size_bytes), '\0'), materializing a zero-filled buffer equal to the file's full logical size. The bytes are never written: preserveFileExtentsAndState (7431) sets file->dataExtents = recovered real runs, and fileDataExtents (1963) returns those, not the buffer. The buffer's SIZE alone carries the logical length via fileLogicalSize (1957), which returns file.data.size() when logicalSizeOverride==0. applyPreservedInodeState (7301) sets logicalSizeOverride ONLY for resource-fork (7325) and sparse (7346) files, never for a plain regular file, so for a plain file the size must come from data.size(). logicalSizeOverride is exactly the mechanism used to avoid holding bytes in RAM for streamed inserts (1402-1407, 9031). collectFullFsTree is invoked on real container images for every in-place op (20+ callers, lines 20966-22820), so the allocation is reachable on any rename/move/delete/insert. Not guarded, not design-frozen; the proposed fix (empty data + logicalSizeOverride = entry.size_bytes, incl. clone paths) is correct and matches existing patterns.
- Fix: In collectDirectorySubtree leave .data empty and set .logicalSizeOverride = entry.size_bytes for regular files so logical length is preserved without allocating the payload; apply the same to any clone-source size derivation (prepareCloneSource reads file.data.size(), so it must also read logicalSizeOverride via fileLogicalSize).

### P03-04 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_executor.cpp:101

- Trigger: User requests an encrypted APFS format; the TEMP volume (QDir::tempPath) is full/failing when the buffered credential write is flushed. write()/flush()/close() fail silently, leaving a 0-byte sak-apfs-cred-*.tmp; StagedScriptCredentials::ok() returns true; the CLI reads an empty password; prepareFormatEncryption() sees isEmpty() and formats the volume as PLAINTEXT while the batch reports success. Thus a truncated/empty credential file passes silently: empty -> plaintext format of a volume the user asked to encrypt; short -> encryption under a wrong password. No guard compares on-disk bytes to the entered secret length before formatting.
- Fix: Check that write() returned secret.size(), that flush() and close() succeeded; on any failure set m_ok=false with an error so ok() aborts before any format command runs. Optionally re-read the staged file and compare length to the secret before substituting the path.

### P02-10 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_ext_file_system_reader.cpp:427

- Trigger: Open a META_BG ext4 image (incompat bit 0x0010 set, small s_first_meta_bg - e.g. a large volume grown past its reserved GDT blocks by online resize) and browse a file whose inode lives in a block group >= s_first_meta_bg. groupDescriptorOffset() returns the flat-table offset for that group, which under meta_bg points at file data / the wrong descriptor, yielding a wrong inode-table block and garbage/incorrect file content with no fail-closed blocker.

There is no catch-all for unknown incompat bits, so a META_BG volume passes validateSuperblock(). groupDescriptorOffset() (lines 520-530) always assumes one flat descriptor table at block 1 (or 2 for 1KiB blocks) + group*group_desc_size, which is only correct for the non-meta_bg layout. For groups at/beyond s_first_meta_bg the descriptors are relocated into their meta block groups, so readInode() (lines 486-499) fetches an unrelated block as the group descriptor and derives a bogus inode-table address. Overflow is guarded (checkedMul/checkedAdd) and out-of-range reads fail via readAt, so the outcome is silent wrong inode data or a spurious blocker rather than a crash.
- Fix: Either implement s_first_meta_bg descriptor placement in groupDescriptorOffset(), or add META_BG (0x0010) to appendUnsupportedIncompatBlockers() to fail closed; ideally reject any unrecognized incompat bit.

### P02-12 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_hfs_file_system_reader.cpp:139

### P03-11 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_script_builder.cpp:2590

Remove-Partition then destroys the source, making the original owners/ACLs/audit settings permanently unrecoverable.

Line 4416 then `Remove-Partition -DiskNumber %1 -PartitionNumber %2 -Confirm:$false` deletes the source, and line 4418 resizes the target into the freed space, so the original security descriptors are gone with no recovery path and no pre-delete verification. This same file uses /COPYALL everywhere else (Invoke-SakRobocopy at lines 1767 and 2468), confirming Merge is an unintended deviation, not a design choice. No fail-closed guard or ACL check exists in the Merge path.
- Fix: Change /COPY:DAT to /COPYALL (equivalently /COPY:DATSOU) and /DCOPY:DAT to /DCOPY:DATE, matching the Invoke-SakRobocopy helper, so ACLs/owner/auditing are preserved before the source partition is removed.

### P03-18 [MEDIUM] src/core/partition_script_builder.cpp:4634
**BitLocker recovery password is exposed in command lines**

- Trigger: User runs a BitLocker Unlock operation with recovery_password="123456-...-...". At execution the 48-digit recovery password appears in cleartext in (a) the powershell.exe -Command argument and (b) the spawned manage-bde.exe command line; any concurrent admin/EDR process enumerating process command lines (or Sysmon EID 1 logging) captures the credential capable of unlocking the volume.
- Evidence: src/core/partition_script_builder.cpp:4633-4638: for BitLockerUnlock, out.script embeds `$recoveryPassword = %1` then `manage-bde.exe -unlock $mountPoint -RecoveryPassword $recoveryPassword` via `.arg(quotePowerShell(recoveryPassword))`, inserting the 48-digit recovery password as plaintext. Redaction at 4639-4641 targets only out.dry_run_script (preview), NOT the executed out.script. Execution path (src/elevated/elevated_helper_main.cpp:319-325, elevatedPowerShellArgs) runs the whole script through `powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command <command>`, so the plaintext `$recoveryPassword = '...'` assignment is in the powershell.exe command line, and PowerShell expands the variable into the manage-bde.exe child-process command line. Both are visible to Win32_Process/WMI enumeration and captured by command-line telemetry (e.g. Sysmon Event ID 1) for the duration. Not in any of the guarded categories.
- Fix: Use the in-process `Unlock-BitLocker -MountPoint $mountPoint -RecoveryPassword $recoveryPassword` cmdlet (keeps the secret in PowerShell memory, no child-process command line) and/or deliver the script via -File/stdin instead of -Command so the assignment is not on the powershell.exe command line.

### P05-43 [MEDIUM] src/core/pdf_email_writer.cpp:96
**Existing files mask PDF write failure**

- Trigger: On Windows, re-export an email whose sanitized filename matches an existing PDF in target_dir that is currently open in a viewer (exclusive lock) or marked read-only. QPdfWriter cannot open the file for writing, doc.print() produces nothing, but fi.exists() is true, so writeMessage() returns full_path as success and m_bytes_written is bumped by the stale file's size - the write failure is silently masked and the old PDF is reported as a fresh export.
- Evidence: src/core/pdf_email_writer.cpp:96-99 determines success with only `QFileInfo fi(full_path); if (!fi.exists())`. QPdfWriter (line 81) opens its backing QFile lazily in WriteOnly at paint/begin; if the destination is locked or read-only, `doc.print(&writer)` (line 94) writes nothing and leaves any pre-existing file intact. Because success is exists()-only, the stale file is returned as a successful export and line 101 `m_bytes_written += fi.size()` counts the old file's bytes. The de-dup map (lines 67-73) only tracks within-session collisions, so an on-disk file from a prior run reuses the same filename and collides. No QSaveFile, device-error check, busy check, or newly-written-size validation guards this in current code.
- Fix: Render through a checked QFile/QSaveFile (verify open succeeds and commit succeeds) or capture the file's pre-write state and require a newly written, nonempty file (e.g., check mtime advanced and size > 0) before returning success.

### P05-44 [MEDIUM] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1156
**ANSI PST encryption is read from the Unicode offset**

- Trigger: Open a legacy ANSI PST (data_version kAnsiVersion, <=2GB, Outlook 2002 or earlier) encoded with NDB_CRYPT_PERMUTE: bCryptMethod=0x01 lives at byte 465, but the code reads byte 513 (a garbage byte beyond the 512-byte ANSI header). encryption_type is misread as ~0x00, so the permute cipher is never applied and every data block parses as garbage (messages/attachments unreadable).
- Evidence: pst_parser.cpp:124 defines kHeaderEncryptionOffset=513 and line 1156 reads data[513] unconditionally for both formats. bCryptMethod at 513 is Unicode-only. For ANSI (32-bit) PSTs the field sits at offset 465 (root@0xA4=164 is 40B ->204; dwAlign 4; rgbFM 128; rgbFP 128 ->464; bSentinel@464; bCryptMethod@465); the ANSI header is only 512B so byte 513 is past the header. The parser fully supports ANSI (parseHeaderRootPointers line 1174 reads ANSI root at 0xA4; isKnownDataVersion accepts kAnsiVersion line 259), so ANSI files reach data-block decode. Reading the wrong byte means an encoded ANSI PST's crypt method is misdetected. (Report's stated ANSI offset 461 is imprecise; correct value is 465, but the not-513 claim holds.) The only encryption-offset test (test_pst_parser.cpp:413) checks 513 for Unicode only, so ANSI is untested.
- Fix: Select the offset by format: use 465 (ANSI) when !m_is_unicode and 513 (Unicode) otherwise, e.g. int off = m_is_unicode ? 513 : 465; read data[off]. m_is_unicode is already set in parseHeaderPreamble before parseHeaderEncryption is called.

### P05-45 [MEDIUM] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1306
**Forged B-tree entry size can read outside the page**

- Trigger: A legacy (non-4K) Unicode PST whose NBT or BBT leaf page metadata sets entry_count=255 and entry_size(cbEnt)=2. Entry index 243 sits at page offset 486; readNodeLeafEntry reads parent_node_id (uint32) at 486+24=510, spanning bytes 510-513 and reading 2 bytes past the 512-byte page buffer allocated by readBytes.
- Evidence: localReadLE (src/core/pst_parser.cpp:267-272) does a raw std::memcpy with NO bounds check. The BTree leaf loop (loadNodeBTree line 1304-1313, loadBlockBTree 1343-1352) only guards `off + entry_size > meta_offset` (line 1307/1317/1346/1356) and caps iterations by the untrusted entry_count; it never validates entry_size against the fixed record layout. readNodeLeafEntry (line 744) reads parent_node_id as uint32 at off+kNbtParentNodeOffsetUnicode(24), ending at off+28, regardless of entry_size. parseBTreePage performs NO minimum-entry-size validation. For a legacy Unicode file (m_is_unicode, !m_is_4k): page_size=512, trailer_size=16 -> trailer_offset=496; meta_pad=4, meta_size=4 -> meta_offset=488. entry_count is a single byte (line 1283, max 255) and entry_size a single byte (line 1284), both attacker-controlled and independent. With entry_count=255 and entry_size=2, idx=243 gives off=486; the guard 486+2=488 is NOT >488, so the entry is processed; parent_node_id reads bytes 510..513, and indices 512,513 lie past the exactly-512-byte page buffer -> 2-byte heap over-read. Caveat: the report's literal cbEnt=1 does not trigger on legacy pages because the single-byte entry_count caps off at 254 (safe); entry_size=2 (or 3) is the real trigger. The 4K format is safe (40 bytes slack after meta_offset > 28-byte read span). Mechanism as described is genuinely unguarded; read-only over-read (crash/DoS), so severity reduced from HIGH to MEDIUM.
- Fix: In parseBTreePage, validate entry_size against the required minimum fixed record size for the page type and level before iterating (Unicode NBT leaf needs >=28 bytes for the off+24 uint32 parent read; BBT leaf >=18), and reject the page if entry_size is below that minimum. Alternatively bound each fixed read against data.size() in the leaf readers.

### P05-47 [MEDIUM] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1475
**Cyclic data trees cause unbounded recursion**

- Trigger: A crafted PST/OST where a data-tree block X has the internal flag set (bid & 0x02), byte[1] == kDataTreeXBlockLevel, entry_count >= 1, and one child bid entry equals X (self-reference) or forms an X->Y->X cycle of internal blocks. Any parse path reaching it (e.g. extractAttachmentFromSubnode 1047, readTableContext, readSingleAttachment 2690, loadNodeHeapContext 2558) calls readDataTree(X), which recurses X->X->... with no depth/visited guard until the native stack overflows and the process crashes (DoS on untrusted input).
- Evidence: readDataTree (src/core/pst_parser.cpp:1420) takes no depth param and keeps no visited-BID set. When bid has the internal flag (kBidInternalFlag, 0x02), it reads the block and, for block_level == kDataTreeXBlockLevel/XxBlockLevel, calls readXblockChildren (1461) / readXxblockChildren (1489), which read child bids straight out of the block bytes and recurse via readDataTree (lines 1475, 1500). The header (include/sak/pst_parser.h:249) confirms the signature has no depth argument. By contrast every other B-tree walk in this file (loadNodeBTree 1294, loadBlockBTree 1333, readSubNodeBTree 2097, parseBTreePage) carries an int depth checked against kMaxBTreeDepth (lines 1248, 2098) precisely to stop this; readDataTree was left unguarded. A child_bid that equals its parent bid (or any cycle) re-reads an identical block reporting the same XBLOCK level and children, so recursion never terminates and never decreases -> native stack exhaustion / process crash. The in-code comment at 1470-1472 documents that internal-flagged child bids are deliberately re-expanded through readDataTree, so the cyclic path is reachable, not defended.
- Fix: Thread an int depth param through readDataTree/readXblockChildren/readXxblockChildren and reject depth > kMaxBTreeDepth (matching loadBlockBTree), and/or track visited bids in the active traversal and reject repeats, returning error_code::pst_corrupted_btree.

### P05-50 [MEDIUM] src/core/pst_parser.cpp:2780
**Failed attachments shift later attachment indices**

- Trigger: A PST message whose subnode B-tree has two attachment subnodes (NID type kNidTypeAttachment) where the first's data tree / BTH is malformed so readSingleAttachment returns std::unexpected (e.g. readDataTree(data_bid) or collectBthLeafData fails), and the second parses cleanly. readAttachments then returns a one-element vector whose att.index = 0 describes the SECOND attachment. When the user loads that attachment, loadAttachmentContent(msg, 0) -> readAttachmentData(msg, 0) walks subnodes with found_count starting at 0, matches attachment_index 0 on the FIRST (malformed) subnode, and returns extractAttachmentFromSubnode of the wrong (first) subnode instead of the second, silently yielding wrong/failed attachment content under the second attachment's displayed name.
- Evidence: Two enumerations of the same attachment subnodes count differently. readAttachments (pst_parser.cpp:2771-2787) increments the exposed index att_index ONLY on readSingleAttachment success (line 2783), so att.index / vector position is a compacted index that skips failed attachments. readAttachmentData (pst_parser.cpp:1096-1107) resolves attachment_index against found_count, which increments for every physical attachment subnode (line 1102-1104) irrespective of parse success, then returns extractAttachmentFromSubnode(sub_it.value()) for the matching physical ordinal. loadAttachmentContent (line 990-1001) passes the vector position (compacted index) straight into readAttachmentData as if it were a physical ordinal. Both loops filter identically on sub_type == kNidTypeAttachment; the only divergence is the success gate, so when an earlier attachment fails to parse the two indices misalign and the wrong subnode's bytes are extracted.
- Fix: Make the two enumerations agree: either have readAttachmentData use the same compacted counter (increment only when a subnode would parse successfully), or store the physical subnode ordinal (or the subnode key/BID) on PstAttachmentInfo and have readAttachmentData look up by that stable identifier rather than re-deriving a positional index.

### P06-10 [MEDIUM] src/core/quick_action.cpp:37
**Cancellation races worker-owned state**

- Trigger: User presses Cancel while an action is executing: the GUI thread runs cancelCurrentAction() -> action->cancel(), which reads m_status==Running and writes m_status=Cancelled via setStatus, at the same moment the worker thread inside execute() writes m_status (e.g. setStatus(ActionStatus::Running) at reset_network_action.cpp:116 or finishWithResult on completion). Two threads read/write the non-atomic m_status with no synchronization -> data race, and cancel()'s Q_EMIT statusChanged fires from the GUI thread on a QObject owned by m_execution_thread.
- Evidence: quick_action.cpp:37-43 cancel() reads non-atomic m_status and calls setStatus(Cancelled) which writes m_status + Q_EMIT statusChanged. It is invoked cross-thread from the GUI thread via QuickActionController::cancelCurrentAction() (quick_action_controller.cpp:334-335) while the action object lives on m_execution_thread after moveToThread() (quick_action_controller.cpp:450) and execute() runs there. execute() and base helpers (emitCancelledResult/finishWithResult/emitFailedResult) also read/write m_status via setStatus on the worker thread (e.g. reset_network_action.cpp:116). m_status is a plain enum in quick_action.h:322 (only m_cancelled is std::atomic, line 325); no mutex/queued connection guards it. Result: concurrent non-atomic read/write of m_status (data race / UB) plus a cross-thread Q_EMIT statusChanged issued from the GUI thread against a worker-owned QObject.
- Fix: Make cross-thread cancel() set only the atomic m_cancelled flag; leave all m_status/result transitions to the worker thread, which already polls isCancelled() (reset_network_action.cpp:112/122) and can emit the Cancelled status/result itself. Alternatively route cancel() through a queued connection so it executes on the worker thread.

### P06-15 [MEDIUM] src/core/quick_action_controller.cpp:413
**Cancelled actions remain cancelled forever**

- Trigger: User executes a QuickAction (e.g. Generate System Report), clicks Cancel mid-run -> cancel() sets m_cancelled=true. Later the user triggers the SAME action again: startExecutionWorker moves it to a thread and calls execute(), whose first line if(isCancelled()) is true, so it emitCancelledResult() and returns without doing any work. Every subsequent scan/execute of that action is silently cancelled for the rest of the process lifetime.
- Evidence: resetCancelled() is defined at include/sak/quick_action.h:264 but has ZERO call sites in the tree (repo-wide grep for resetCancelled returns only the header definition). cancel() (src/core/quick_action.cpp:37-43) sets the atomic m_cancelled=true and nothing ever clears it. The controller's re-entry paths -- scanAction (quick_action_controller.cpp:205), executeAction (:230), startScanWorker (:413), startExecutionWorker (:441) -- start the reused action without resetting the flag. Each action's scan()/execute() short-circuits on the flag, e.g. generate_system_report_action.cpp:49-53 immediately emits emitCancelledResult and returns when isCancelled() is true. Since QuickAction objects are persistent members (getAction returns from m_actions and are reused across runs), a once-cancelled action is permanently stuck cancelling until app restart.
- Fix: Call action->resetCancelled() at the top of startScanWorker() and startExecutionWorker() (or in scanAction/executeAction before dispatch) so each fresh run begins with a clear cancellation flag.

### P06-41 [MEDIUM] src/core/script_rewriter.cpp:134
**URL filename is not escaped for PowerShell**

- Trigger: A Chocolatey install script whose resource URL ends in a filename containing a single quote, e.g. https://example.com/Bob%27sTool.zip (or a literal https://example.com/a';Start-Process calc;'.exe). buildLocalFilenameMap() sets filename = QUrl::fileName() (default PrettyDecoded, so %27 -> '), which is passed unmodified to buildToolsPath() and emitted as (Join-Path $toolsDir 'Bob'sTool.zip'). The embedded quote closes the single-quoted string, producing a broken script for benign names and arbitrary PowerShell token injection for a crafted name.
- Evidence: src/core/script_rewriter.cpp:134 buildToolsPath returns QString("(Join-Path $toolsDir '%1')").arg(filename) with no quote-doubling or basename validation. The filename originates unescaped from QUrl::fileName() at src/core/package_internalization_engine.cpp:531 (and :42), inserted into local_filenames and used verbatim by replaceUrl()/buildToolsPath(). QUrl::fileName() percent-decodes %27 to a single quote and preserves literal single quotes in the last path segment, so the value can contain PowerShell-significant characters. The rewritten script is written to disk (rewriteToFile) for execution, so the unescaped single quote breaks/escapes the single-quoted string. No guard, validation, or escaping exists on this path in current code.
- Fix: In buildToolsPath, validate filename as a bare basename (reject path separators) and double every embedded single quote (filename.replace("'", "''")) before interpolating into the single-quoted PowerShell string.

- Fix: Move m_totalBytesToCopy = calculateTotalSize() (lines 114-119) before the checkDiskSpace() call (line 107) so the space check has a real required size.

### P06-32 [MEDIUM] src/core/user_profile_restore_worker.cpp:153
**Partial restore reports success**

- Trigger: Restore a user whose backed_up_folder source path exists but the destination disk is full (or ACL-protected): every QFile::copy at line 363 fails, m_filesErrored is incremented per file, no files are written, yet run() reaches line 153 and emits restoreComplete(true, summary) reporting overall success.

Failure paths are only logged, never propagated: restoreUser() returning false is merely logged (lines 134-136); copyFileWithConflictResolution() return value is discarded by the caller at line 330; per-folder buildSafePath/restoreFolder failures continue after logging (lines 193-206). The only success exits before 153 are validateBackup() failure and cancellation. So a run where the source folder is missing, permissions are denied, or the disk fills mid-copy leaves files unrestored yet reports success=true to the wizard. The summary string does include the error count, but the boolean the UI keys off is always true. Not guarded anywhere.
- Fix: Base the final success flag on error state, e.g. emit restoreComplete(m_filesErrored == 0, summary) (or track a required-failure flag from restoreUser/copy results) so any unrestored required file yields success=false.

### P06-33 [MEDIUM] src/core/user_profile_restore_worker.cpp:430
**Existing file is deleted before replacement is secured**

- Trigger: ConflictMode=KeepNewer, destination file exists and is OLDER than the source (or KeepLarger with destination SMALLER). remove() succeeds, then QFile::copy(source, finalDestPath) fails because the source becomes unreadable mid-restore or the volume runs out of space. Result: the pre-existing destination file is gone and no replacement was written, losing any unique content that destination held.
- Evidence: src/core/user_profile_restore_worker.cpp: KeepNewer branch line 430 QFile::remove(finalDestPath) and KeepLarger branch line 447 QFile::remove(finalDestPath). finalDestPath is initialized to dest (line 344) and is NOT reassigned in either branch, so remove() deletes the actual destination file. The replacement copy is performed only afterward at line 363 (QFile::copy(source, finalDestPath)). QFile::copy will not overwrite an existing file, so the destination must be removed first; but there is no temp-sibling staging, QSaveFile, or atomic rename. If the subsequent copy fails, the original destination is already permanently deleted. No guard exists in the tree for this path.
- Fix: Stage the replacement into a temporary sibling (e.g. finalDestPath + ".sak_tmp"), verify the copy succeeded (and optionally verifyFile), then atomically replace the destination (QFile::remove old + QFile::rename temp, or QSaveFile). Only delete the original after the new copy is fully written.

### P06-35 [MEDIUM] src/core/user_profile_restore_worker.cpp:52
**Running restore thread can be destroyed**

- Trigger: Restore a profile whose backup contains a large file (e.g. a 20GB+ Outlook .ost or video). While the run() thread is inside QFile::copy for that file (well over 5s), close the restore wizard/dialog or quit the app. ~UserProfileRestoreWorker sets m_cancelled but QFile::copy cannot observe it; wait(5000) times out and is ignored; ~QThread runs on the still-active thread and the process aborts via std::terminate.
- Evidence: src/core/user_profile_restore_worker.cpp:52-57: ~UserProfileRestoreWorker() calls cancel(); wait(kTimeoutThreadTerminateMs) and discards the wait() result. kTimeoutThreadTerminateMs = 5000 (layout_constants.h:94). cancel() (line 88-91) only sets atomic m_cancelled; the run loop checks it between files/folders (lines 115,184,315) but NOT during the blocking, uninterruptible QFile::copy at line 363. The worker is a QObject child of the wizard page (new UserProfileRestoreWorker(this), user_profile_restore_wizard_execute.cpp:133), so tearing down the page/dialog while a copy is in flight invokes the destructor. If a single file copy exceeds 5000ms, wait() returns false (ignored) and the base ~QThread destroys a still-running thread -> "QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running" -> std::terminate. The bounded wait is a partial mitigation, not a guard.
- Fix: In the destructor, retain the worker until run() actually finishes: wait() without a timeout (or loop wait() until it returns true) so the thread is joined before ~QThread; alternatively make QFile::copy cancellable via chunked copy that polls m_cancelled so cancel() can bound the wait reliably.

### P07-48 [MEDIUM] src/core/wifi_analyzer.cpp:486
**Channel utilization merges different Wi-Fi bands**

- Trigger: Scan environment with a 2.4 GHz AP on channel 1 and a 6 GHz AP that also maps to channel 1 (freq 5955 MHz -> channel 1). Both hash to key 1: networkCount becomes 2, their RSSIs are averaged together, and the reported band is whichever was iterated last, producing a single bogus utilization/interference entry instead of two distinct per-band entries.
- Evidence: src/core/wifi_analyzer.cpp:486 declares `QHash<int, WiFiChannelUtilization> channelMap;` keyed only by `net.channelNumber` (line 493). `util.band = net.band;` (line 495) is a non-key field overwritten by whichever network is processed last, and `util.averageSignalDbm += net.rssiDbm` (line 498) accumulates across all bands sharing that channel number, then averages (line 507). No band component in the key -> cross-band merge. The collision is real per the file's own frequencyToChannel: 6 GHz channels are 1,5,9,... (line 445) colliding with 2.4 GHz channels 1-13 (line 435), and 6 GHz 36-165 collide with 5 GHz numbers.
- Fix: Key aggregation by (band, channelNumber) pair, e.g. QHash keyed on a composite or QHash<QString,...> using band+channel.

### P07-49 [MEDIUM] src/core/wifi_profile_scanner.cpp:91
**Wi-Fi backup stores plaintext display text as XML**

- Trigger: Back up a WPA2 profile with PSK "Secret123": scanAllWifiProfiles stores the key=clear console text (containing "Key Content : Secret123") into xml_data, and saveWifiProfilesToBackup writes it verbatim into plaintext wifi_profiles.json - the password is exposed in cleartext on disk, and because the stored text is console output rather than WLANProfile XML it also cannot be re-imported via `netsh wlan add profile filename=`.
- Evidence: src/core/wifi_profile_scanner.cpp:91-95: `netsh wlan show profile name=<n> key=clear` stdout is assigned to `info.xml_data`. That command emits human-readable console text (with a `Key Content : <plaintext-PSK>` line), not `<WLANProfile>` XML. src/gui/user_profile_backup_wizard_execute.cpp:184-196 serializes `xml_data` verbatim (p.toJson(), user_profile_types.cpp:459) into an unencrypted plaintext `wifi_profiles.json` in the backup destination. Two real defects: (1) the cleartext Wi-Fi key is stored in an ordinary JSON backup artifact; (2) the blob is not importable WLAN XML, so the restore path that uses `netsh wlan add profile filename=` (wifi_manager_panel.cpp:2092) would reject it. Neither is guarded/encrypted anywhere in the flow.
- Fix: Use `netsh wlan export profile name=<n> key=clear folder=<dir>` to emit the real importable XML, store those XML files, and protect the credential-bearing artifacts (encrypt at rest / restrict ACLs) since key=clear embeds the plaintext keyMaterial.

### P07-50 [MEDIUM] src/core/windows_usb_creator.cpp:278

Diskpart tokenizes "select disk 1\nselect partition 1\nactive\n" and reads the disk argument as "1\nselect" (invalid), never executing "active". The partition is never marked active, so verifyBootableFlag() at line 310 fails and the operation aborts at STEP 4 VERIFICATION FAILED on an already-wiped/populated USB. Fix: replace "\\n" with "\n".
- Evidence: src/core/windows_usb_creator.cpp:278-282 builds the diskpart script with C++ string literals "select disk %1\\n" "select partition 1\\n" "active\\n". In C++ source, "\\n" is a two-character escape producing a literal backslash followed by 'n' (NOT a newline). The script written to the temp file at line 284-285 is therefore the single physical line: select disk 1\nselect partition 1\nactive\n (with \n as literal text). Contrast the correct sibling cleanAndPartitionDisk() at line 360-367 which uses single-backslash "\n" (real newlines) and works. Live path confirmed: run() line 76 -> configureBootAndVerify() line 322 -> setAndVerifyBootFlag() line 345.
- Fix: Change the three "\\n" occurrences at src/core/windows_usb_creator.cpp:279-281 to "\n" to match the working cleanAndPartitionDisk() script format.

### P04-18 [MEDIUM] src/core/xz_decompressor.cpp:17
**Legacy LZMA files use an XZ-only decoder**

- Trigger: Feed a valid legacy .lzma (LZMA-alone) file - one beginning with 0x5D 0x00 0x00 (the default lc/lp/pb properties byte followed by dict-size bytes), or simply named foo.lzma. detectFormat returns "xz", the factory builds XzDecompressor, initStream succeeds with lzma_stream_decoder, then decompressStep() -> lzma_code returns LZMA_FORMAT_ERROR (7), surfacing "Decompression error: lzma error code 7" and aborting the flash/read of an otherwise valid file. Fix: use lzma_auto_decoder (handles both .xz and .lzma) in initStream.
- Evidence: decompressor_factory.cpp explicitly advertises legacy LZMA-alone support: line 77 maps the ".lzma" extension to format "xz", and line 123 maps the LZMA-alone magic {0x5D,0x00,0x00} to "xz". Both are routed to XzDecompressor (line 40). XzDecompressor::initStream() at xz_decompressor.cpp:17 calls lzma_stream_decoder(&m_lzmaStream, UINT64_MAX, 0). lzma_stream_decoder decodes ONLY the .xz container format, not the legacy .lzma alone format; the line-16 comment claiming "Automatic format detection (XZ or LZMA)" is false (that behavior belongs to lzma_auto_decoder). A genuine LZMA-alone file therefore fails with LZMA_FORMAT_ERROR at the first lzma_code() call in decompressStep() (line 51). No guard or alternate decoder handles this path.
- Fix: Replace lzma_stream_decoder with lzma_auto_decoder in XzDecompressor::initStream (xz_decompressor.cpp:17), which auto-detects and decodes both .xz and legacy .lzma-alone streams, matching what the factory already advertises.

### P10-02 [MEDIUM] src/gui/ai_assistant_panel.cpp:9903
**Active session can change during an in-flight run**

When A's response arrives, onResponseReady -> handleAssistantResponse -> persistAssistantResponse writes A's Assistant transcript and token usage into session B's store (currentSessionId() == B), and traces/memory/context land in B's stores as well; A's run-state snapshot has also been clobbered by loadRunStateSnapshotForSession. When the user picks a different item, onSessionSelected calls m_conversationStore->openSession(session_id) (9914), which changes the store's current session, then also resets m_tokenTracker, reloads trace store (refreshTraceStoreForSession), and overwrites run state (loadRunStateSnapshotForSession). All run-completion persistence resolves the destination via the store's *current* session at callback time with no run->session binding: persistAssistantResponse (10607) -> appendTranscript(\"Assistant\") + writeUsage (10611-10619); handleAssistantResponse (10550); appendSessionMemory (7829), persistContextItem (7786) and command appends all gate on / write to m_conversationStore->currentSessionId(). Notably the sibling New-session (9963) and Rename (4393-4400) controls ARE busy-gated with isAiBusy(), confirming the combo path was overlooked rather than intentionally left open. The combo connect (3625-3628) is a plain currentIndexChanged->onSessionSelected with no guard.
- Fix: Reject session switching while isAiBusy() (early-return in onSessionSelected and revert combo index) and/or disable m_sessionCombo in updateCredentialControls when busy, mirroring the New-session/Rename gating; and bind each run's persistence to the originating session id captured at run start rather than currentSessionId() at callback time.

### P10-03 [MEDIUM] src/gui/ai_assistant_panel.cpp:2048
**HTTPS downloads have no size limit**

- Trigger: The model invokes runDownloadTool with an https URL whose server streams a multi-gigabyte (or unbounded chunked, Content-Length omitted) response. Since only the scheme is checked, the reply accumulates in QNetworkReply's internal buffer and readAll() (line 2048) materializes the entire body in RAM within the 5-minute window, growing until the process is OOM-killed. No byte limit aborts the transfer.
- Evidence: src/gui/ai_assistant_panel.cpp:2048 - downloadUrlBytes() does state.payload = reply->readAll() with no Content-Length check and no running byte cap. The whole reply is buffered into a QByteArray on the QNetworkReply::finished handler; the only limit is kDownloadTimeoutMs = 5*60*1000 (line 2060). Caller runDownloadTool() (line 6102) validates only url.isValid() and scheme==https (line 6111); the URL is supplied by the AI tool call, and the response body size is entirely server-controlled. No QSaveFile streaming, no maximum-size guard, no downloadProgress abort exists anywhere in the path (bytes flow readAll -> writeDownloadBytes -> QCryptographicHash). Finding is unguarded in current code.
- Fix: Cap accepted size: on the reply connect a readyRead/downloadProgress slot that aborts once bytesReceived exceeds a fixed limit, and stream incrementally into a QSaveFile instead of buffering readAll() then writing.

### P10-06 [MEDIUM] src/gui/ai_assistant_panel.cpp:10566

### P12-05 [MEDIUM] src/gui/email_calendar_dialog.cpp:1479
**Event text can corrupt or inject ICS properties**

- Trigger: Right-click a meeting-request event whose body has more than one line (e.g. "Agenda:\nDiscuss Q3"), choose "Export Event as ICS". After the detail loads body_plain, the DESCRIPTION line emits the raw newline, so "Discuss Q3" becomes a separate unrecognized content line and the exported VEVENT is malformed/invalid. Same corruption occurs if the subject or location contains a newline, or a literal backslash/semicolon/comma that RFC 5545 requires to be escaped.

No escaping/line-folding helper exists anywhere in src/gui (grep for IcsEscape/escapeText/foldLine returns no matches). evt.body_plain is populated at line 1561 from detail.body_plain (the plain-text body of a calendar/meeting item), which realistically contains CR/LF. Raw CR/LF written into a property value violates RFC 5545 content-line rules and corrupts the VEVENT.
- Fix: Add an RFC 5545 TEXT-escaping helper (backslash-escape \\, ;, ,, and replace CR/LF with \n) plus 75-octet line folding, and apply it to subject, location, and body_plain before writing each property.
