### P11-05 [MEDIUM] src/gui/file_explorer_properties_dialog.cpp:77
**Large APFS files receive mislabeled prefix hashes**

- Trigger: Open Properties on a raw-target (non-local) APFS/HFS+/ext regular file larger than 512 MiB and open the Hashes tab. The reader returns the first 512 MiB with ok=true plus a discarded truncation warning; the displayed MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256 digests are computed over only that 512 MiB prefix but are labeled as the file's digest, so a user comparing digests could wrongly conclude two files are identical when they differ only past 512 MiB.
- Evidence: computeHashes (file_explorer_properties_dialog.cpp:77-101) calls FileManagementFileSystemBridge::readFile(target, path, kPropertiesHashMaxBytes=512 MiB), checks only read.ok, and hashes read.data while ignoring read.warnings. For raw non-local targets, readFile (file_management_file_system.cpp:831-856) routes to the readers, which truncate oversized files and return ok=true. Confirmed in partition_apfs_file_system_reader.cpp:926-931 (and 885-889 for compressed): when inode->size > bytesToRead it appends warning "APFS file read truncated at N bytes" but leaves ok=true (result.ok = result.blockers.isEmpty(), a warning is not a blocker) and returns only the 512 MiB prefix. The dialog then displays MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256/CRC32 over that prefix labeled as the file's digest, with no truncation indication. This is an oversight not a design choice: the bridge's own hashFile (line 878-890) requests max_bytes+1 and sets a capped flag, and writeRawSourceOut (940-956) does the same; the dialog bypasses hashFile. Local files instead fail closed (readLocalFile line 388-392 appends a blocker + ok=false over the limit), so the mislabeling is specific to raw APFS/HFS/ext targets.
- Fix: In computeHashes, request kPropertiesHashMaxBytes+1 (mirroring hashFile/writeRawSourceOut), detect data.size() > cap as capped, and either reject the incomplete read or label the values as prefix/partial digests; alternatively route through the existing FileManagementFileSystemBridge::hashFile which already tracks the capped flag.

### P11-10 [MEDIUM] src/gui/file_management_explorer_panel.cpp:3891
**Undo can delete an unrelated replacement**

- Trigger: Copy file A to a raw APFS/HFS target at path P (records a File history item). Before undoing, replace P with a different file B of the same name (still a regular file) - via S.A.K. itself or another writer. Undo the Copy: destinationEntryKind returns File, matches item.directory==false, and historyDeleteOneEntry permanently deletes B through the certified raw writer, destroying an unrelated file the copy never created.
- Evidence: src/gui/file_management_explorer_panel.cpp:3891 historyDeleteOneEntry checks only PasteEntryKind (File vs Directory) via destinationEntryKind (line 3241-3266), which is derived solely from existence + isDir. FileExplorerHistoryItem (include/sak/file_explorer_storage_history.h:28-32) stores only source_path, destination_path, directory - no size/mtime/hash/inode. Thus a same-kind replacement at the same path passes the guard and is deleted; on raw targets (lines 3912-3915) via certified deleteFile/deleteDirectoryTree it is permanent. The comment at 3887-3890 claims same-named user replacements 'must not be deleted' but only kind swaps are actually detected, so the identity guarantee is unfulfilled.
- Fix: Journal a stable identity for each created/copied entry (e.g. size + mtime, or content hash / inode where available) at record time and refuse the undo deletion when the current occupant does not match it, rather than relying only on path + File/Directory kind.

### P11-13 [MEDIUM] src/gui/file_management_explorer_panel.cpp:7505
**Eject leaks an exclusively locked volume handle**

- Trigger: User right-clicks a removable/non-system drive (e.g. E:) in the file explorer sidebar and selects Eject. lockVolume opens \\.\E: and acquires FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME, dismountVolume succeeds, ejectLocalTargetAtIndex returns at 7516 without CloseHandle(volume). Each such eject leaks one kernel volume handle and keeps the lock held. Repeating for the same or other volumes accumulates leaked handles and can keep a volume locked/inaccessible for re-mount until the process terminates.
- Evidence: In ejectLocalTargetAtIndex (src/gui/file_management_explorer_panel.cpp:7503-7517), `HANDLE volume = unmounter.lockVolume("\\\\.\\E:")` opens the volume with GENERIC_READ|WRITE and applies FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME (drive_unmounter.cpp:185-207). The handle is used only for `unmounter.dismountVolume(volume)` and is NEVER passed to CloseHandle nor inserted into the unmounter's owned-handle map. The DriveUnmounter destructor (drive_unmounter.cpp:62-70) closes ONLY handles stored in m_lockedVolumes; that map is populated exclusively by lockAndDismountVolume (line 144: m_lockedVolumes.insert), which the eject path does NOT call. Both exit paths leak: success (7514-7516 return) and dismount-failure (7507-7512 return, handle still valid since lockVolume succeeded). The stack-local `unmounter` is destroyed at function end with an empty m_lockedVolumes, so the handle survives, held open (with its FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME lock) until the long-lived GUI process exits.
- Fix: Call CloseHandle(volume) on both the success and failure paths (or wrap in an RAII guard), matching lockAndDismountVolume which closes on failure and hands ownership to m_lockedVolumes on success.

### P12-09 [MEDIUM] src/gui/image_flasher_panel.cpp:1045

- Trigger: Select ubuntu-24.04-live-server-amd64.iso and a USB target, click Flash. Filename contains "server" so isWindowsInstallISO() returns true; the USB is NTFS-formatted (createBootableUSB STEP 1) and 7z-extracted, then STEP 2 fails ("No install.wim or install.esd found"). Result: wiped, non-bootable USB and no raw flash of the Linux ISO.

So a Linux server ISO gets formatted+extracted and then errors out, never raw-flashed.
- Fix: Remove the generic "server" (and ideally the whole filename) heuristic; determine Windows-vs-raw by inspecting ISO contents for sources/install.wim or install.esd before choosing the format-based Windows workflow, so Linux ISOs fall through to raw flashing.

### P11-14 [MEDIUM] src/gui/log_viewer.cpp:187
**Failed saves truncate existing logs and report success**

- Trigger: User clicks Save Log and selects an existing file (e.g. a prior log) as the destination on a nearly-full or failing volume. If the destination is an existing file the user picks to overwrite, the WriteOnly open already truncates it; a mid-write failure leaves it destroyed/partial while success is reported.
- Fix: Write via QSaveFile, check out.status()==QTextStream::Ok and saver.commit(), and only show "Save Complete" after commit() returns true; otherwise show a Save Error.

### P09-03 [MEDIUM] src/gui/partition_manager_panel.cpp:4166
**Off-volume backup validation trusts drive-letter text**

- Trigger: Target cluster-size change on volume T:. User picks backup directory E:\backup-link where E:\backup-link is an NTFS junction into T:\stash (or equivalently T: is also mounted as folder C:\Mounts\Data and user picks C:\Mounts\Data\backup). clusterBackupIsOffTarget: targetPrefix 'T:', backup does not start with 'T:', returns true (off-target => allowed); downstream changeClusterBackupOnTargetVolume/targetPathStartsWithDrive likewise passes. Backup files land physically on T:, then T: is reformatted, destroying the only backup -> data loss.
- Evidence: Both the UI accept-gate and the downstream safety validator confine the backup destination purely by lexical drive-letter prefix, with no physical-volume resolution. src/gui/partition_manager_panel.cpp:4166-4169 clusterBackupIsOffTarget builds targetPrefix = volume.drive_letter.left(1).toUpper()+":" and returns !backup.startsWith(targetPrefix). Same lexical pattern in backupIsOffAllocatedVolumes (4232) and backupIsOffPartitionVolume (4249). Downstream src/core/partition_safety_validator.cpp targetPathStartsWithDrive (473-480) plus normalizedBackupDirectory (438-442, only replaces '/'->'\\') back changeClusterBackupOnTargetVolume (524) and allocationBackupOnTargetOrDonor (1095). No GetVolumePathName, no volume GUID/handle resolution, no QFileInfo::canonicalFilePath, no reparse-point/junction handling anywhere. A destination that physically lives on the target volume but is reached via a different drive letter (junction/symlink, or the target volume also mounted as a folder on another lettered volume) fails the startsWith(T:) test, is classified off-target, and is allowed; the backup is written onto the target and destroyed by the subsequent reformat.
- Fix: Resolve the backup path to its owning volume before destructive work: GetVolumePathNameW to get the mount root, then GetVolumeNameForVolumeMountPointW / volume GUID, and compare that GUID against the target/donor volume GUID instead of comparing drive-letter text prefixes.

### P09-07 [MEDIUM] src/gui/partition_manager_panel.cpp:10930
**Queued restore image has a destructive TOCTOU window**

- Trigger: Select target Disk 1, choose restore image C:\backup.img (1 GiB), confirm the queue prompt. Before clicking Apply, another process (or the user) truncates/replaces backup.img: (a) truncate to 100 MiB -> Apply offlines Disk 1, writes ~100 MiB, then throws at premature EOF, leaving the physical target partially overwritten and non-bootable; or (b) replace with a different 1 GiB image -> Apply silently restores the wrong content and full/sample verification (re-reading the swapped source) reports success.
- Evidence: Queue path onRestoreImage (src/gui/partition_manager_panel.cpp:10930-10976) validates the image via QFileInfo (exists/isFile/size) only at queue time, then persists only source_path plus the queued source_size_bytes into the payload. No stable handle, Windows file ID, or content digest is retained. Apply-time re-validation in partition_safety_validator.cpp (blocksTooSmallCloneTarget:892, restoreImageMissingKnownSizes:901) reads only the queued payload sizes and never re-stats or re-hashes the file. Execution in partition_script_builder.cpp cloneTransferExecutionScript (line 2151) opens $src by PATH with FileShare::ReadWrite, sets $expectedBytes to the queued size, then Copy-SakBytes (line 2105) streams that many bytes into the offlined physical target BEFORE any short-read check. On a truncated source it throws 'Source ended before expected byte count' only after the target has been partially overwritten. cloneTransferVerifyExecutionScript (line 2164) re-reads the same on-disk $src, so a same-size content swap is written and then verified against itself and passes. - Fix: Before opening the target for write, re-open the source once, capture a stable handle plus BY_HANDLE_FILE_INFORMATION (file index/volume) and current length, compare against queued metadata, and stream from that single retained handle through completion (optionally verifying a digest captured at queue time).

- Evidence: src/gui/user_profile_backup_wizard_pages.cpp:2201-2211 (onSelectAll) rebuilds EthernetConfigInfo from table cells assigning only adapter_name, dhcp_enabled, ip_address, subnet_mask, default_gateway, selected -- never dns_primary/dns_secondary (nor description). This is the only path that stores non-empty configs into the wizard via setEthernetConfigs (line 2214); populateTable (2130) and onScanEthernet (2075) do NOT store configs, and there is no itemChanged/validatePage collector for this page (grep confirms only 2214 and 2230 call setEthernetConfigs). So DNS scanned+displayed is silently dropped to empty strings in ethernet_configs.json and lost on restore. The comma-joined DNS text sits in column kEthernetColumnDns (=6) but is never parsed back.

### P12-28 [MEDIUM] src/gui/vulnerability_panel.cpp:679

The QTextStream buffer is not even flushed until the stream destructor runs at the closing brace (line 680), which is AFTER the success log is emitted. The sibling onExportJson() at lines 712-718 demonstrates the codebase's own standard: it checks file.write(bytes) != bytes.size() and reports a short write. The CSV path omits that guard, so a partial/truncated write is reported as success.
- Fix: After the write loop, call out.flush() and check out.status() != QTextStream::Ok (or file.error() != QFileDevice::NoError); on failure emit the same showWarningLogged path used by onExportJson instead of the success log. ### P12-31 [MEDIUM] src/gui/wifi_manager_panel.cpp:1237

### P12-32 [MEDIUM] src/gui/wifi_manager_panel.cpp:1367
**Truncated credential JSON is reported saved**

Both compute `if (f.write(json_bytes) != json_bytes.size())` and only call `sak::logWarning(...)` on a short write. Neither returns nor changes the outcome: control falls straight through to `f.close()` and then `Q_EMIT statusMessage(QString("Saved %1 ... network(s) to %2"))`. Plain QFile (WriteOnly) truncates the destination on open, so a partial write leaves a corrupt/truncated JSON while the UI still reports success. No QSaveFile, no failure return, no user-facing warning on short write.
- Fix: Write via QSaveFile; treat a short write (write() != size) or a failed commit() as failure - return early and emit an error status instead of the "Saved" success message, so the prior good file is not clobbered by a partial write.

### P07-54 [MEDIUM] src/threading/flash_worker.cpp:150
**Unbuffered raw I/O uses an unaligned Qt buffer**

- Trigger: Flash any image whose byte size mod 4096 != 0 (e.g. a size that is a 512-multiple but not a 4096-multiple) to a native 4Kn target device (4096-byte LOGICAL sector). The full 64 MB buffer writes succeed, but the final partial WriteFile at flash_worker.cpp:306 is padded only to a 512 boundary by padBufferToSectorSize (kDeviceSectorSizeBytes=512, line 31/236). Under FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING (line 155) both the length (e.g. 1536) and the resulting file offset must be integer multiples of the LOGICAL sector size (4096 on a 4Kn drive); a 512-multiple-but-not-4096-multiple length/offset returns ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER (87), so writeImage() returns false and the entire flash is reported failed.
- Evidence: flash_worker.cpp:155 opens the device with FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING; line 31 hardcodes kDeviceSectorSizeBytes=512 and padBufferToSectorSize (lines 230-266) rounds the trailing partial buffer up to a 512 boundary only. There is NO device-geometry query anywhere (no IOCTL_DISK_GET_DRIVE_GEOMETRY_EX / IOCTL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY STORAGE_ACCESS_ALIGNMENT), so the actual logical sector size is never consulted. On 4Kn devices FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING requires length and offset to be multiples of 4096, which the 512-based padding violates for the final partial write. The report's broader "modern devices / heap alignment" framing overstates impact: (a) 512e drives (virtually all modern USB/SSD/HDD) present 512-byte LOGICAL sectors, and offset/length enforcement is against the logical sector size, so the 512-padding is compliant there; (b) \\.\PhysicalDriveN disk devices' Direct-I/O AlignmentRequirement is typically permissive, so the plain 16-byte-aligned QByteArray buffer.data() (lines 276/285) does not fail in practice, which is why the shipped tool works on common hardware. Failure mode is loud (writeImage returns false, WriteFile error logged at line 312), not silent corruption. Genuine unguarded gap but narrow (rare 4Kn removable targets), hence MEDIUM rather than HIGH.

### P07-55 [MEDIUM] src/threading/flash_worker.cpp:306
**Successful partial raw writes are silently accepted**

- Trigger: Flash an image larger than the target device (no capacity-vs-image-size check in validateTargets or writeImage) with verification set to Skip. At the device-full boundary the final WriteFile returns TRUE with bytesWrittenThisTime < bytesRead; m_bytesWritten ends up short but writeImage() returns true, so the drive is reported as flashed successfully with the trailing sectors never written. The same silent acceptance occurs on a device with a pending-remap/failing sector that produces a short write mid-image, corrupting all subsequent offsets.
- Evidence: src/threading/flash_worker.cpp:305-321: WriteFile success is checked, but bytesWrittenThisTime is never compared to bytesRead; the code does m_bytesWritten += bytesWrittenThisTime and loops to the next read. Since m_imageSource->read() already advanced the source by the full bytesRead (line 285), a short write drops the chunk's tail and misaligns all following writes. No final m_bytesWritten == m_totalBytes check exists (line 331 only logs, 332 returns !stopRequested()). No upstream guard: FlashCoordinator::validateTargets (src/core/flash_coordinator.cpp:330-381) checks only handle open + IOCTL_DISK_GET_DRIVE_GEOMETRY, never comparing device capacity to image size, so an oversized image is not blocked. Verification is the only backstop and is optional (ValidationMode::Skip / m_verificationEnabled false, flash_worker.cpp:338-344).

### P04-38 [LOW] include/sak/input_validator.h:331
**Mixed-sign `safeCast` comparisons are incorrect**

- Trigger: safeCast<uint64_t>(int64_t{1}) returns std::unexpected(integer_overflow) instead of 1, because static_cast<int64_t>(UINT64_MAX) == -1 makes the check `value > -1` true.
- Evidence: include/sak/input_validator.h:331-332 casts To's limits into From before comparison: `value < static_cast<From>(numeric_limits<To>::min()) || value > static_cast<From>(numeric_limits<To>::max())`. For signed->unsigned widening this is wrong. safeCast<uint64_t>(int64_t{1}): static_cast<int64_t>(UINT64_MAX)==-1, so upper check is `1 > -1` -> true -> rejected as integer_overflow. Every positive int64_t->uint64_t is wrongly rejected. No guard present. std::in_range<To>(value) would be correct. Impact is low because grep shows safeCast is only called in tests/unit/test_input_validator.cpp, and only via the both-signed (int64->int) path which does not hit the defect; no production callers.

### P08-17 [LOW] src/ai/ai_conversation_store.cpp:1185
**Short writes are reported as successful persistence**

- Trigger: Volume hosting the AI session directory reaches ENOSPC (or hits a quota) mid-append: file.write() writes fewer bytes than the JSON payload (or returns -1), the trailing newline is lost/partial, appendJsonLine returns true, and the caller proceeds as if the JSONL record was durably written - leaving a truncated line that breaks subsequent line-oriented parsing of that artifact.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_conversation_store.cpp:1185-1188 appendJsonLine() calls file.write(QJsonDocument(object).toJson(QJsonDocument::Compact)); file.write("\n"); then returns true with no check of either write() return value, no flush(), and no close/error check. QFile write in buffered Text mode can short-write or fail on disk-full without surfacing until flush; the destructor flush ignores errors. Unlike the adjacent writeManifest() (lines 1147-1162) which uses QSaveFile and checks commit(), this append path reports success even when the record is truncated. appendSearchIndexRecord (1204) and message/memory appends route through this same function, so callers treat a partial line as durably persisted.
- Fix: Capture the return of each write(), require it to equal the payload length, call flush() and check its result (or use QSaveFile + commit() as writeManifest does), and return false with an error_message on any short/failed write.

### P08-18 [LOW] src/ai/ai_human_gate_store.cpp:39
**Approval history can be silently lost or corrupted**

- Trigger: Disk full (or I/O error) while appendGate writes an approve/resolution record for an existing pending gate: file.write returns -1/short but appendGate returns true; on next process start loadGates skips the malformed partial line, latest_by_id for that gate_id resolves to the older pending record, and latestPendingGate re-presents the already-approved gate for approval.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_human_gate_store.cpp:39-41 - appendJsonLine calls file.write(...) twice and returns true with no return-value/flush/close-error check (no QSaveFile, no bytes-written comparison). appendGate:79 propagates that unconditional success. loadGates:98-99 silently `continue`s past any record with a JSON parse error or non-object, surfacing nothing. Combined with latestPendingGate:106-124 (last-record-wins per gate_id, then newest-pending returned): a resolution record written during disk-full/I/O error is reported successful but is partial/absent; the corrupt trailing line is skipped on reload, so the gate's surviving latest record is its earlier pending entry and latestPendingGate returns it as pending again.
- Fix: Check both file.write return values against the buffer size, call file.flush() and verify, and return false with error_message on any short/failed write; in loadGates, treat a non-empty line that fails to parse as store corruption (surface via error_message) rather than silently continuing.

### P08-10 [LOW] src/ai/ai_mcp_stdio_client.cpp:199
**MCP output can exhaust process memory**

- Trigger: A configured MCP stdio server that, once started, writes a continuous newline-free byte stream to stdout (e.g. while(1){write(1MiB)}) or spews unbounded stderr while stalling its stdout JSON response. canReadLine() stays false / stderr is never read, so QProcess's unlimited internal read buffer grows every readyReadStandardOutput cycle until the timeout fires; with a sufficiently large timeout_ms the process buffers many GB and can OOM the app before the timer kills the process tree.
- Evidence: src/ai/ai_mcp_stdio_client.cpp: no QProcess::setReadBufferSize() is ever called, so the stdout read buffer is unlimited. handleReadyRead()/processLine() (lines 198-220) consume stdout only via canReadLine(); a subprocess emitting bytes with no newline accumulates unbounded in the QProcess buffer. stderr is never drained during normal operation - processStderr() (line 102) is only invoked from timeoutMessage()/exitedMessage() on timeout/exit, so stderr also accumulates unbounded. The only backstop is the single-shot timeout timer armed at line 168 (qMax(timeout_ms, 1000ms)), which bounds elapsed time but NOT memory: within the (caller-controlled) timeout window, buffers can grow without limit. No byte cap or incremental drain exists, so this is not guarded against the described scenario.
- Fix: Call m_process->setReadBufferSize(cap) to bound stdout, connect readyReadStandardError to drain stderr incrementally into a capped ring buffer, and fail-closed (abort + kill tree) if either stream exceeds a strict byte cap.

### P08-26 [LOW] src/ai/ai_trace_store.cpp:202

### P04-35 [LOW] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:273
**Maximum-results limit can be exceeded**

- Trigger: Set max_results=50. File A contains 40 matches: total=40 (<50, continue). File B contains 40 matches: searchFile returns all 40 (its own per-file cap of 50 not hit); processSearchFile does total_matches += 40 -> 80, batch_matches now holds 80 matches, then 80 >= 50 stops. The worker has emitted/returned 80 matches, exceeding the requested limit of 50 (overshoot bounded by ~2*max_results-1 since a single file is itself capped at max_results).
- Evidence: src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:273-277 (processSearchFile) appends the whole file's match batch via batch_matches.append(matches); total_matches += matches.size(); and only then checks total_matches >= m_config.max_results. searchFile (lines 647, 675, and other collect sites) caps an individual file at max_results, not at the remaining allowance. So the running total is only compared to the limit after a full file's batch is added, allowing overshoot. This is a soft/lagging cap, not a hard one; no remaining-allowance is threaded into per-file search.
- Fix: Thread a remaining = max_results - total_matches allowance into searchFile/processSearchFile and truncate each file's returned batch to that remainder before appending.

### P04-36 [LOW] src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1901
**Binary-search byte offsets are UTF-16 indexes**

- Trigger: A binary/text file whose bytes are C3 A9 20 58 ("e-acute", space, "X") searched with regex /X/. QString::fromUtf8 yields "e-acute space X" so capturedStart()==2 and is reported as line_number/offset 2, but X's true byte offset is 3 (off by one). The hex context window computed as capturedStart()-16 .. capturedEnd()+16 is likewise sliced from the raw byte array at the wrong position, yielding a misaligned Hex: dump. Any additional multibyte codepoint before a match increases the drift.
- Evidence: src/core/advanced_search_worker.cpp:1901 in searchBinary(): content is read as raw bytes and decoded via QString::fromUtf8(content) (line 1893). regexMatch.capturedStart() returns a UTF-16 code-unit index into that decoded QString, but it is stored as match.line_number with the literal comment "// byte offset" (line 1901) and reused unchanged to slice the raw QByteArray for hex context (lines 1907-1910: content.mid(start, end-start)). Because each non-ASCII UTF-8 sequence (2-4 bytes) collapses to 1-2 UTF-16 units, the index no longer equals the byte offset, so both the reported offset and the hex context slice are shifted/misaligned. No mapping from UTF-16 position back to UTF-8 byte offset exists; nothing guards this. Identical defect at searchBinaryBytes lines 1933-1944.
- Fix: Map regex QString positions back to UTF-8 byte offsets before reporting or slicing, e.g. textContent.left(capturedStart()).toUtf8().size() for the offset, and slice hex context using UTF-8 byte offsets of capturedStart()/capturedEnd().

### P07-11 [LOW] src/core/diagnostic_report_generator.cpp:33
**CSV output permits formula injection**

- Trigger: A USB/storage device reporting a model or serial string beginning with a formula character (e.g. dev.model = "=HYPERLINK(\"http://evil\",\"x\")" or "=1+1") is inventoried; the CSV report is generated and opened in Excel/LibreOffice with formula auto-evaluation, executing the formula. Quoting by csvEscape does not prevent evaluation.
- Evidence: src/core/diagnostic_report_generator.cpp:33 csvEscape() only applies RFC 4180 quoting: it wraps in quotes and doubles embedded quotes when the value contains ',', '"', or '\n' (lines 34-39), and otherwise returns the value unchanged. It never prefixes leading '=','+','-','@' with an apostrophe. Call sites at lines 356-400 pass hardware/WMI-derived strings (cpu.name, dev.model, dev.serial_number, dev.interface_type, gpu.name, gpu.driver_version, os_name) directly through csvEscape into the CSV. A cell like =HYPERLINK(...) is emitted verbatim (or quoted, which still evaluates in Excel).
- Fix: In csvEscape, if the first character is one of '=','+','-','@' (and optionally tab/CR), prepend a single quote (') before applying RFC 4180 quoting. Low severity: local offline diagnostic tool, requires victim to open the CSV in a formula-evaluating spreadsheet.

Real and unguarded, but severity is LOW because the collision requires a file named exactly "sak_disk_benchmark.tmp" at the drive root -- an obviously tool-specific temp name -- and fixed-name-in-root is standard disk-benchmark practice.

### P04-25 [LOW] src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:112
**APFS container-size multiplication can wrap**

- Trigger: Raw APFS partition device (isRawDevicePath) with no caller-supplied size, whose block-0 NXSB superblock has nx_block_size=4096 and nx_block_count=0x10000000008000 (2^52+32768). probeApfsContainerBytes computes 4096*(2^52+32768) = 2^64+2^27, wraps to 2^27 = 128 MiB. 128 MiB is >= 64 MiB and <= 24 TiB, so apfsWriteSupported becomes true and the bridge marks the corrupt ~18 EB container as writable instead of rejecting it as an absurd/unknown size.
- Evidence: src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:112 `return block_size * block_count;` is an unchecked quint64*quint64 multiply. block_count comes straight from the on-disk nx_block_count (offset 0x28, line 106) and is only checked !=0 (line 109); block_size is bounded 4096..65536 (lines 107-108). No overflow guard and no validation against actual device length. probeApfsContainerBytes() feeds target.size_bytes (line 688), which is range-gated at lines 204-205 against kMinimumApfsGeneratedContainerBytes=64 MiB and kMaximumApfsGeneratedContainerBytes=24 TiB (include/sak/partition_manager_types.h:37,41). A crafted nx_block_count wraps into that window and passes the write-capability gate.
- Fix: Before multiplying, reject block_count > UINT64_MAX / block_size (or use a checked/builtin overflow multiply), and optionally clamp/validate the derived size against the real device length. On overflow return 0 so the gate fails closed.

### P04-26 [LOW] src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:249

- Evidence: Confirmed at src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:249 vs 657.
- Fix: In applyCapabilities, preserve the inbound read_only: e.g. target.read_only = target.read_only || !target.local_file_system, and fold actual volume read-only into can_write_files (can_write_files = can_write_files && !target.read_only).

### P04-28 [LOW] src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:393
**Local reads bypass limits and swallow I/O errors**

- Trigger: Read a file located on flaky/removable media (e.g., a USB stick pulled, or an SMB/network share that drops) while readAll() is in progress: readAll() returns the partial buffer read before the I/O error, QFile::error() is set, but readLocalFile ignores it and returns result.ok=true with the truncated data. The preview consumer treats the partial content as a complete, successful read. Secondarily, a file grown by another writer between the size() check (388) and readAll() (393) is returned in full, exceeding maxBytes.
- Evidence: src/core/file_management_file_system.cpp:380-396 readLocalFile: line 388 checks file.size()>maxBytes, line 393 result.data=file.readAll(), line 394 result.ok=true unconditionally. There is no QFile::error() check after readAll() and no bounded read. QFile::readAll() returns the bytes read so far on an I/O error, so a mid-read failure yields partial data marked ok=true. The size-before-readAll pattern is also TOCTOU. Path is read-only preview via FileManagementFileSystemBridge::readFile (line 831-835), so impact is a truncated preview shown as complete, not data loss -> LOW rather than MEDIUM.
- Fix: Read at most maxBytes+1 via bounded read loop (or check file.atEnd()/bytesAvailable), then check file.error()!=QFileDevice::NoError and reject with a blocker; reject when read size exceeds maxBytes to close the TOCTOU growth window.

### P06-40 [LOW] src/core/migration_report.cpp:235
**Export reports success after write failure**

### P02-09 [LOW] src/core/partition_apfs_file_system_reader.cpp:2078
**APFS block-offset multiplication can wrap**

Then (2^44+1)*1048576 = 2^64 + 2^20 wraps mod 2^64 to byte offset 1048576 = block index 1. readBytes seeks to 1048576 (in-device on even a tiny image), reads block 1, whose valid Fletcher-64 checksum passes, so the reader accepts block 1's unrelated metadata as the object that was supposed to live at paddr 2^44+1.

The only guard is line 2071 `if (block >= blockCount_ && blockCount_ != 0)`, confining block < blockCount_. But blockCount_ is read raw at line 587 (le64) and NEVER validated against device size or an upper bound; validateBlockGeometry (600-608) bounds only blockSize_ (kMaxApfsBlockSize=2^20). No device_->size() or blockCount_*blockSize_ check exists in the file. Thus block can approach 2^64 and the product wraps mod 2^64 to a small in-range offset. readBytes (2127) bounds length<=INT_MAX and offset<=qint64max, but a wrapped-small offset passes both, seeks in-device, and validateObjectBlockChecksum (2110) accepts any self-consistent Fletcher-64 object without cross-checking the expected oid/paddr. The extent path added an explicit anti-wrap guard (lines 1953-1957) but the generic readBlock path did not. Severity adjusted MEDIUM->LOW: read stays device-bounded and checksum-gated with no OOB/memory-safety impact (exact-length QByteArray copy); impact is metadata aliasing against a crafted image only.

### P01-05 [LOW] src/core/partition_apfs_writer.cpp:6007

- Fix: Before the loop, reject nkeys that cannot fit: require kApfsBtreeNodeHeaderBytes + nkeys*kApfsBtreeFixedTocEntryBytes <= valueAreaEnd (TOC region) and validate each key/value span lies within [keyAreaStart, valueAreaEnd]; append a blocker and return empty on violation. Apply the same cap in readOmapIndexChildPaddrs and readOmapSingleEntryPaddr.

### P01-12 [LOW] src/core/partition_apfs_writer.cpp:19054
**Image creation can truncate a raced existing file**

- Trigger: Caller invokes buildImageOnlyFormatImage with image_path=/some/out.dmg. imagePathIsSafeForCreate passes because the path does not yet exist. Before createSizedApfsImage runs image->open(QIODevice::WriteOnly), another process creates /some/out.dmg with valuable content. The WriteOnly open truncates that file to zero and resize() overwrites it; if a subsequent blocker fires (e.g. buildImageOnlyFormatImageWithSeedFile seed-file path), QFile::remove(result.image_path) then deletes the foreign file entirely.
- Evidence: imagePathIsSafeForCreate (partition_apfs_writer.cpp:4705-4710) rejects an already-existing path via a non-atomic QFileInfo::exists() check. createSizedApfsImage (line 19054) then opens with QIODevice::WriteOnly, which truncates rather than create-exclusive. This is a real check-then-open TOCTOU: the existence guard does not cover the window between the check and the open. On later failure paths (e.g. seed-file blockers at 20805/20817/20833) cleanup calls QFile::remove(result.image_path), so a raced-in file would be both truncated to zero and then deleted. The fix (QIODevice::WriteOnly | QIODevice::NewOnly, supported in Qt 6.10) makes the open create-exclusive. Severity adjusted down from HIGH to LOW: this is a local single-user disk utility with a user-chosen output path and no privilege boundary; exploitation requires another process to create a file at the exact chosen path within the sub-millisecond check-to-open window.
- Fix: Open with QIODevice::WriteOnly | QIODevice::NewOnly (Qt 6.10) so creation fails atomically if the path already exists, and ensure failure-cleanup only removes a file this handle actually created.

### P02-15 [LOW] src/core/partition_ext_file_system_reader.cpp:602
**Entry cap can produce a silently incomplete export**

- Trigger: Export a flat ext directory (e.g. the export root "/") containing exactly options.max_entries + 1 regular files. processFrame requests budget=max_entries; readDirectoryRecords returns max_entries records, silently dropping the (max_entries+1)th (line 601-604, no blocker). processEntries consumes all max_entries slots (entries_scanned ends at max_entries), pending_ empties, and run() returns result_.ok=true with one file missing and no "Export entry cap reached" blocker.
- Evidence: src/core/partition_ext_file_system_reader.cpp:601-604 readDirectoryRecords breaks at records.size()>=maxEntries and appends NO blocker on truncation. Export path: processFrame (line 1023-1024) calls listDirectory with budget=max(1, options_.max_entries - result_.entries_scanned). The export's cap detector consumeEntrySlot (1068-1075) only appends "Export entry cap reached" when it attempts to consume a slot with entries_scanned>=max_entries. Because the listing is pre-truncated by readDirectoryRecords to exactly the remaining budget, the export processes exactly budget entries and never attempts the (budget+1)th slot, so the blocker never fires. run() then sets result_.ok = blockers.isEmpty() = true (line 1011). Result: a file is silently omitted while ok=true. Not guarded elsewhere: processFrame never compares the returned entry count to the requested budget, and listDirectory sets ok=true on a truncated listing.
- Fix: Request budget+1 records in processFrame (or have readDirectoryRecords accept a limit of maxEntries+1) and treat receiving more than the budget as truncation: append a blocker / set ok=false, or propagate an explicit truncated flag on PartitionExtFileReadResult so listDirectory reports incompleteness.

### P04-37 [LOW] src/core/regex_pattern_library.cpp:275
**Pattern persistence truncates before successful save**

- Trigger: User has an existing custompatterns.json with N saved patterns. A save is triggered (add/edit a pattern) while the target volume is full or hits a write error. open(WriteOnly) truncates the old file to 0 bytes; file.write() then returns a short count, logError fires, and the function returns - the previous valid library is gone and the file contains truncated/partial JSON that will fail to parse on next load. QSaveFile would keep the original intact until a successful commit.
- Evidence: src/core/regex_pattern_library.cpp:275-287, saveCustomPatterns(). QFile file(m_storage_file); file.open(QIODevice::WriteOnly | QIODevice::Text) opens the EXISTING library file directly. WriteOnly truncates the file to zero length at open() time, before any new bytes are written. There is no QSaveFile, no write-to-temp-then-rename, and no atomic commit. The subsequent short-write check (line 283) only logs after data is already lost. So if file.write() fails partway (disk full, quota, I/O error) or the process dies mid-write, the previously valid pattern library is already destroyed and only partial/empty JSON remains on disk.
- Fix: Replace QFile with QSaveFile(m_storage_file): open(WriteOnly), write json_bytes, and only commit() on full success; on short write call cancelWriting() so the original file is left untouched.

### P06-24 [LOW] src/core/user_data_manager.cpp:652
**Recursive copy follows junctions outside source roots**

- Trigger: A directory junction inside a source root, e.g. C:\Users\Username\Documents\ext -> C:\SomeUnrelatedTree (created via `mklink /J`). When backing up Documents, copyDirectory lists 'ext' as a normal subdir, recurses, and QDir resolves it to C:\SomeUnrelatedTree, copying that entire foreign tree into the backup. A junction pointing to an ancestor (e.g. Documents\loop -> C:\Users\Username\Documents) causes deep repeated recursion that copies the tree many times until the lengthening dest path makes mkpath fail.
- Evidence: src/core/user_data_manager.cpp:652-663: copyDirectory enumerates subdirs via source_dir.entryList(QDir::Dirs | QDir::NoDotAndDotDot) (no QDir::NoSymLinks) and recurses unconditionally through copyDirectory(source_subdir,...). No QFileInfo::isSymLink()/reparse-point rejection and no containment check that source_subdir stays under the original source root. grep of the whole file for NoSymLinks|reparse|symlink|isSymLink|junction returns zero matches, so the behavior is unguarded. On Windows QDir treats junctions/symlinks as ordinary directories, so QDir(source_subdir).entryList transparently lists the junction TARGET and the copy follows it. Source roots are getStandardDataPaths() (Documents/Home/AppData), user-writable places where a junction can legitimately live. Mitigating factors keeping severity low: single-user local backup tool, and a self-referential/parent junction self-terminates because the growing destination path eventually fails dest_dir.mkpath('.') (line 633) rather than looping forever.
- Fix: Skip reparse points/symlinks: check QFileInfo(source_subdir).isSymLink() (and on Windows also test FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT) before recursing, or add QDir::NoSymLinks to the entryList filter, and/or canonicalize each entry and require it stay under the original source root.

### P06-43 [LOW] src/core/windows_user_scanner.cpp:59

### P12-02 [LOW] src/gui/app_installation_panel_table.cpp:260
**Truncated queue file reported as saved**

- Trigger: Disk exhaustion (or quota limit) while writing a large install queue: QFile::write() flushes fewer bytes than json_bytes.size(), leaving a truncated/invalid-JSON file on disk. Only a warning is logged; the status bar and log still report "App list saved (N packages)". The user believes the queue was saved, but a later loadQueueFromFile() on that file fails JSON parse (parseQueueFile returns false at line 310-316).
- Evidence: src/gui/app_installation_panel_table.cpp:260-267, saveQueueToFile(). Line 260 `if (file.write(json_bytes) != json_bytes.size())` detects a short write but line 261 only calls `sak::logWarning("Incomplete write...")` with no early return. Control falls through to `file.close()` (263) and then unconditionally emits success: `logOutput("Saved %1 package(s)...")` (265) and `statusMessage("App list saved (%1 packages)")` (266-267). No QSaveFile/commit() and no fail path. So a truncated write is reported to the user as a successful save.
- Fix: Write via QSaveFile: write the bytes, verify write()==size and flush()/commit() succeed, and only emit logOutput/statusMessage success after commit() returns true; on any failure show the existing Save Failed warning and return. This also avoids leaving a partially-written file over a previously-good one.

### P11-03 [LOW] src/gui/file_explorer_breadcrumb.cpp:68
**UNC paths collapse into local-root paths**

- Trigger: Breadcrumb path set to UNC '\\server\share\folder'; user clicks the 'share' (or 'server') ancestor segment. Emitted target is '/server/share' instead of '//server/share', so navigation goes to a bogus local-drive path rather than the network share.
- Evidence: src/gui/file_explorer_breadcrumb.cpp:65-82 splitPathSegments(). Line 67 normalizes '\\server\share\folder' to '//server/share/folder'. Line 68 tests only startsWith('/') and line 69 seeds accumulated as "/". Line 73 split() uses Qt::SkipEmptyParts, dropping both leading empty tokens from '//', so parts=["server","share","folder"]. The loop rebuilds targets as "/server", then "/server/share", then "/server/share/folder" - every UNC segment target loses the required double-slash. Clicking 'share' at line 118 emits segmentActivated("/server/share"), which Windows resolves as a local-root path, not the network share \\server\share. No UNC handling or guard exists in the function.
- Fix: Detect a UNC prefix before splitting (normalized.startsWith("//")): seed accumulated with "//" and build the first two segments (server, share) as the UNC root "//server/share", then append remaining segments normally.

No guard, validation, or completeness propagation exists.

### P11-06 [LOW] src/gui/file_explorer_properties_dialog.cpp:229
**Enabling a hash during calculation leaves it blank**

- Trigger: Open Properties on a single large regular file, switch to the Hashes tab so the default CRC32/MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256 pass starts computing (QtConcurrent::run in flight). Before it finishes, tick the SHA-384 checkbox. The four default digests populate when the pass completes, but the SHA-384 label remains blank (never shows "Calculating...") because the toggle's startHashCalculation() no-ops on the running watcher and the finished handler schedules no follow-up pass. SHA-384 only fills in after another checkbox toggle or tab change.
- Evidence: src/gui/file_explorer_properties_dialog.cpp: toggled lambda (lines 229-234) sets m_hashes_started=false then calls startHashCalculation(); but startHashCalculation() (lines 251-254) early-returns while m_hash_watcher.isRunning() is true. So a checkbox enabled mid-calculation is dropped: its label is never even set to "Calculating..." and stays empty until an unrelated later toggle/tab-switch fires startHashCalculation() again with the watcher idle. Not guarded anywhere.
- Fix: In the m_hash_watcher finished handler (after applyHashes), call startHashCalculation() again so any algorithms enabled while a pass was running get a follow-up pass; startHashCalculation already computes pending from unchecked/empty labels and returns early when nothing is pending, so it is safe to always call.

### P11-15 [LOW] src/gui/log_viewer.cpp:225

### P09-08 [LOW] src/gui/partition_manager_panel.cpp:8839
**Disk map ignores physical segment order**

A disk whose physical layout is partition/gap/partition therefore renders as partition/partition/gap, showing false adjacency. **Rightmost drag position cannot select the maximum**

- Trigger: On a 1 TiB row rendered in a 500-pixel track, drag the right handle to the far-right pixel: bytesForX clamps x to track.right() = left+499, returning 499*total/500 ~= total - 2 GiB. updateDrag sets newEnd to that value (clamp does not raise it to total), so the segment size is stuck ~2 GiB below the true maximum and total_bytes can never be selected by dragging.

In updateDrag Right branch (line 2961) newEnd=std::clamp(selectedBytes,minEnd,total); clamp caps at total but never raises selectedBytes, so a full-right drag lands below total_bytes and the maximum size is unselectable via dragging. Not guarded anywhere.
- Fix: Map the right endpoint using track.width() consistently (e.g. treat the inverse span as track.width(), or clamp x to track.left()+track.width() rather than track.right()), with explicit handling for a one-pixel track.

**QR names permit output-directory traversal**

(dot not replaced), outDir = baseDir + "/.." resolves to baseDir's parent, and the QR image files (e.g. "...png") are written into that parent directory, outside the user-selected folder.
- Evidence: src/gui/wifi_manager_panel.cpp:924 (single export) and :936 (batch, executeSingleQrNetwork) sanitize the QR sub-folder name with replace(QRegularExpression("[\\\\/:*?\"<>|]"), "_"), which strips path separators and Windows-invalid chars but NOT '.' components. outDir is then built as baseDir + "/" + subName and passed to QDir().mkpath (lines 884-885 / 937-938) with no normalization/confinement check that the result stays inside baseDir.
